Draft 10 had fourteen grammar rules and thirteen tasks; so has draft 11. No rule or task is added or removed, and no number moves. Terms gain the field `calls`; Statements lose `received` and `passed_on`. The definitions gain **report** and **call** (named, not defined); **leg report** is defined anew. The answers are unchanged. Sections 8.2 and 8.3 of draft 11 are new: the list left open by its own changes, and what a reader still meets from earlier lists.

## D.2 What the click test showed, and which change answers it

The test is the Dubsar Suite lead's (`docs/click-sketch-report.md`): three split-service cMIPs sketched on draft 10, a reader of rule 11.4, six runs; a film standing on a song and a sound library, a band member three legs from the click. A record of work, not a decision. In plain words: one click can reach everyone, but only if every leg on the way reports, and only if rule 11.4 is read in a way its words did not quite say.

- **Every leg on the path must report, or the people beyond are not shown** (its section 2; run 3). They never chose the silent service, and a payer could not tell before paying. *Draft 11:* not closed. No leg is made to report (S43: "may or may not", "if needed"). What changes is sight: the call is in signed Terms, so a reader sees before paying which legs called (rule 11.2, 11.4(e)); silence is shown as "unanswered" or "no report called for". The rest is a stated cost, and item 2 of draft 11's section 8.2.
- **3a. Rule 11.4 stopped at the first deal** (run 2): it looked for the next report within the agreement a `passed_on` entry named, while a work paid through its deal is reported on within the deal. *Change 3:* a reader follows a leg to where its call says reports are expected (11.4(a)).
- **3b. Nothing linked one report to the next:** the settlement arrives first, the report later. *Change 3:* two reports are tied by the act both name, the settlement; neither needs to know the other (11.4(b)).
- **3c. Disagreement needs amounts, and the shape carried none.** *Change 3:* amounts are part of K8's content; reports that disagree are shown side by side (11.4(c)).
- **3d. Kept or held** (run 4): a payout held under a minimum looked like a fee, "kept 38 of 38". *Change 4:* K8's row asks for what was kept apart from what is still owed.
- **3e. Pools:** one report for a batch works; "my click's share" is the reader's own arithmetic, not stated. *Left open,* P6.
- **3f. Loops** (run 6): money went round until fees and rounding ate it; the reader needed a rule of its own. *Left open,* P8.
- **3g. A person who receives for others** (every run): no report is passed and none is asked; the other owner is never shown. *Left open,* P20.
- **What worked as written** (an agreement as a party; a receiver that is an agreement; every payment and report naming the version it read; shares read by each service's cMIP): unchanged.

## D.3 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the proposal, say more than the exchanges

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check". Each item is something a line, or `report-block-1.md`, says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 11 does. The brief asks draft 11 to use the block as the proposal describes it; so where the proposal goes beyond the exchange, draft 11 follows the proposal for the block, and lists it here.

1. **"if a click cannot be followed without every leg reporting, rule 11 changes"** (Must every leg report?). His words approve the test ("Good idea, test by building on top, I like it"); they do not say what follows from its result. And one thought of his is not in the line: "When needed could be all the time that we did not check". Draft 11 changes rule 11 on the strength of the next exchange, not of this line.
2. **"a block for all of MOR, not only Agreements"** (The report). His words: "Yes, I lean so too." A leaning, as "I lean a." was (section 9.11, item 5).
3. **"the call says from whom (the doer, the receiver, or both) and where each report is expected."** His words: "a task can if needed specify where it expect its report from". One phrase, which the line reads as two things, from whom and where. "The doer, the receiver, or both" is the lead's.
4. **"which direction is the call's to say, not the core's."** His words: "The real question is: is this task unidirectional or bidirectional?", then that this is "the question the spec above answers". Which spec he meant (the rule just stated, a task's own spec, the call) is not said. Draft 11 follows the proposal: the call says from whom, and K8 names the two roles.
5. **"call", as a noun.** His words use the verb ("call for a report") and say that the *task* specifies. That the call is "attached" by an act and "binds only the signers of the act that holds it" is the proposal's. His other word, "confirmations", is used by neither.
6. **"Every report carries the call it answers, the acts it relied on, and who signs it; everything else is the task's."** The three parts are the lead's question; his answer is "Seems enough yes". "Everything else is the task's" is the lead's.
7. **"A report is asked for only by those who signed the act that holds the task."** His words are a question: "Who signed the act that holds the task, no?" "Only" is the lead's. What it means for a deal, where the work never signs, is item 4 of draft 11's section 8.2.
8. **"Each side signs only its own half; an act both name (a settlement) ties the halves."** This is the lead's proposal, put at the start of the exchange. His answer speaks of tracking "both directions" and of agreements that "point to the right places"; it does not take up the settlement. Draft 11 uses the tie because the brief's change 3b asks it, and reads it also between two legs' reports (section 8.2, item 6).
9. **"The agreements need to point to the right places."** In his words the agreements point. In the line and the proposal a call, attached to a task, says where. In draft 11 the call sits in Terms, so the two meet; the field is Claude's (section 8.2, item 3).
10. **"This replaces the leg report's place in rule 11 and answers the Dubsar lead's question in the form '(c), narrowed': no leg is made to report by the core."** The lead's. His words name neither rule 11 nor that question. "May or may not" and "if needed" support that nobody is made to report. "As exercise", of tracking both directions, is his and is in no line.
11. **The proposal, beyond the line:** the three things a verifier shows for halves (matched, mismatched, one side only) and the two for silence (unanswered, no report called for), with their MUSTs; "a report that names no call is no report"; the lie it exposes and whom it protects; that it sits "in the shared conventions, above every layer". All the lead's first wording. Draft 11 names the shown words as the block's, and writes its own lie and protection for rule 11 from them.
12. **"an observation, not a rule... No sweep of the texts follows from it"** (May and can't). Close to the exchange. "No sweep" is the lead's reading of his answer that it is not a bible. One part of his sentence is in no line: "CAN’T for the bad the bytes can check". Draft 11 does nothing with it.

## D.4 Draft 10's list of open items, unchanged, and what draft 11 does with it

What follows is section 8.2 of `draft-10.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 10" there mean draft 10; rule 11 there is draft 10's.

Each item is where one of draft 10's changes touches something the exchanges "Building blocks in draft 9" and "Chain and sequence" do not cover. Each is left as draft 9 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so.

1. **"A payment is divided by the version it was made under."** In plain words: draft 9 had this as part of the rule on paying out, from his words "Only a signed condition can update the agreement on one end". The matching of a pay-out now belongs to the service's cMIP (section 5.1), so that sentence is no longer a grammar rule. What the grammar keeps of it is the blocks: a payment names the version it read (rule 13.2), a later version does not reach back (rule 13.1), and a filling keeps the rights of its own version (rule 10.6). Whether the sentence itself should stand in the grammar, for every leg whatever its cMIP, is not said in the exchange.
2. **The deal, and not the service or its public offer.** His words ("the target is not the split service but the agreement with the split service, not some kind of public offer") are now direction for the service's cMIP, quoted in rule 1. The grammar itself reads any agreement named as a receiver as the next leg, and no longer tells a deal from an offer. A work that names a service's public offer as its receiver is not stopped by the grammar.
3. **A silent deal, a late payment, the fee.** These were read by the grammar (answer: not shown). They are now read by the service's cMIP, and a verifier without it answers unchecked.
4. **Is a leg made to report?** His words: "informing everyone of the whole where needed has its value." The MOR lead's line says each leg reports. Rule 11 defines the report and makes no leg give one; where one is missing, the rest is not shown (11.4). If every leg must report, rule 11 changes.
5. **The shared shape, in detail** (P20): how "what" is written; whether a fee kept is stated; who signs where there is no service; one report for many payments. And the names `received`, `passed_on`, "leg report" are Claude's; "leg" is his word.
6. **Which sequences use the shape.** The exchange speaks of "the whole chain of payment and also the chain of agreements that dictates". Rule 11 is written for any sequence. Rule 14 (the baker) is left as draft 9 has it, apart from the word: whether the agreements of a baker's sequence report in the shared shape is not said (P18).
7. **Reports that do not agree.** Rule 11.4 shows both and chooses neither. Whether that is a fork in the sense of rule 12, and what ends it, is not said.
8. **The agreed witness is no longer required.** Draft 9's grammar said a role rewarded for being seen MUST name its witness, and that only the witness's counts count. That is now the spot's cMIP's (section 5.1). The lie that rule exposed (inflated attention) is exposed only where the cMIP names a witness; written as a cost (section 7). Draft 9 also defined "seen"; draft 10 defines no such word.
9. **Closing with no mark.** The role's `closed` and `closes` are gone. A role names its K12 cMIP by fingerprint, with no parameters of its own; how a new version "says" that a spot takes no new identities (a different cMIP, a parameter, a mark that cMIP reads) is left to that cMIP. Whether a role's cMIP should take parameters, as a rule does, is not said.
10. **One condition, and where it sits** (P21). Draft 9 had `condition` on a version (for rights) and `closes` on a role (for the offer). Draft 10 keeps the version's field for both and drops the role's. Whether the one condition should sit on the version or on the role, and whether a version may name several, is not said.
11. **The power to sign the join.** Draft 9's arbiter was a cMIP whose answer ended a fork by itself, with nobody signing. A power needs someone to sign the join, even where its rule only accepts the join that can be computed. For a fork in one signer's own line, a join signed by someone else under a power is made `for` that signer; how such an act sits in that signer's line (rule 6.1, which names the signer's own previous act) is not said. Task K14 is gone; the tasks are thirteen.
12. **"Chain", where it is neither.** His words: "chain only points at chains of the same act". Three uses in this text are neither the versions of one agreement nor a sequence of agreements, are not covered by the exchange, and are left as draft 9 has them: the name of rule 6, "Own chain" (one signer's own acts in one agreement), with the cost "Ordering and chaining show patterns"; rule 13's "where chains cite each other" (from his "chains crossing"); and rule 10's reasoning, "a deed, a chain, a holding, an identity" (of what is received from a previous holder). This text's own word for the first is "line" (section 2). Renaming is the MOR lead's, as technical wording.
13. **Rule 11 is a new rule under an old number.** In drafts 2 to 9 rule 11 was "Seen, by the agreed witness". To keep every other rule number where the records point, draft 10 gives the freed number to the new block. If that confuses more than a gap would, the numbering is the MOR lead's to change. One clause moved: draft 9's rule 10.8 is rule 10.7.
14. **Words that may be typos.** He asked that suspected typos in his quotes be run by him. Three are quoted here as the ledger has them, uncorrected: "don.t", "watever" and "techincally".
15. **Not brought in.** The ledger also records an aside of his, on a test in which the grant was refused. Nothing is written from it. It may bear on P17.
16. **Nothing was tested.** No test was run against draft 10. Whether one click reaches every contributor is still for a test of cMIPs to show; with the paying details now in a cMIP, that cMIP has to be written before the test can be run.

**What draft 11 does with it.** Item 4 (is a leg made to report?): tested, and answered by S43 in the form "no leg is made to; a reader sees which legs called" (draft 11, section 8.2, item 2, for what that leaves). Item 5 (the shared shape in detail): what is kept is now stated, apart from what is still owed; `received` and `passed_on` are gone; the rest is P20 and section 8.1. Item 7 (reports that do not agree): shown side by side with their amounts; whether it is a fork is still not said. Item 16 (nothing was tested): draft 10 was tested (D.2); draft 11 was not. Item 14: the head of the ledger now lists the three words as corrected with him; the texts still carry them. Every other item stands as written.

**Change 4** is not a change to a rule: draft 12's section 8.2 opens with the three items of draft 11's list that changes 1 to 3 touch (who makes the call in a deal; the tie between two legs; whether sight is enough), and says for each what is answered and what stays open.

Draft 11 had fourteen grammar rules and thirteen tasks; so has draft 12. No rule or task is added or removed, and no number moves. Rule 11 gains two clauses, 11.5 and 11.6. No field is added or removed. No definition is added; **party**, **service**, **deal** and **report** are changed. The answers are unchanged. No parameter is added or removed; P17 and P20 are changed. One cost is new ("One leg, and no further") and one is changed ("A service is a hand").

## E.2 Where each change landed

- **Head and section 0:** the paragraph "And one thing here is this text's alone"; the central idea (it need not sign to be named; where it signs, as its own terms require); the service's deal, signed on both sides; rules 1 and 11 in plain words; the shower.
- **Section 2:** **report** (one leg), **party** (not bound where it has not signed; signs as its own Terms require), **service** and **deal** (signed on both sides).
- **Rule 1:** plain words; 1.2 rewritten; one lie and one protection added; the reasoning's first paragraph, and a new paragraph "Where it signs".
- **Rule 4:** 4.4 rewritten; 4.10, one sentence; the reasoning on the counter signature, its last sentences.
- **Rule 9:** the last sentence of its reasoning.
- **Rule 11:** plain words (the call in a deal; "Every leg is the same exchange"; "One leg"; who can follow a click); 11.2 (where Terms hold the task; the receiver who reports; who makes the call in a deal; no call attached to a receiver); 11.4, its first sentence and (a), (b), (d), (e); 11.5 and 11.6, new; the lie and whom it protects; three new paragraphs of reasoning, and his sentence from the other window.
- **Section 5:** K8's row (a receiver's report confirms); section 5.1, its first row and a sentence after the table.
- **Section 6:** P17, P20.
- **Section 7:** "A service is a hand", one sentence; "One leg, and no further", new.
- **Section 8:** 8.1, the report block's item, one new item beside it, the last item rewritten and one added; 8.2, new; 8.3, brought up to date.

Rules 2, 3, 5 to 8, 10 and 12 to 14 are unchanged.

## E.3 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines say more than the exchanges

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check". Each item is something a line says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 12 does. The brief repeats most of the lines; where it does, draft 12 follows the exchange, and says so.

1. **"The Dubsar Suite lead reads it as settling the silent leg... and most of 3a"** (The same rule). That reading, and the list of what is still open, are the Dubsar Suite lead's and the MOR lead's. His words are one sentence, which restates the block. Draft 12 takes nothing more from the entry.
2. **"every leg is the same exchange"** (A person who receives for others). His words describe one case: a service, a band receiver, the band. "Every leg" is the lead's generalising. Draft 12 follows it (rule 11.5), since he says "the same exchange" himself.
3. **"whoever carries out the obligation reports that they did; the receiver confirms by its own report".** His words say what happened in the case ("reported that it did so", "confirmed it via report"). They do not say that any leg must. Draft 12 keeps the earlier "may or may not" (section 8.2, item 8).
4. **"(no call attached to a receiver; that proposal is withdrawn)".** He did not refuse the proposal in words: he answered "So the report comes from the bandmate receiving", and then described the exchange. The withdrawing is the lead's, of his own proposal.
5. **"In the grammar a report travels one leg, and no further."** His words: "One leg, that’s all it can do." They answer the lead's proposal about how far a confirmation travels upstream. "In the grammar" and "no further" are the lead's. What "one leg" covers in rule 11.6 is Claude's reading (section 8.2, item 12).
6. **"Anything beyond one leg... is for data cMIPs focused on agreements, which people agree to in an agreement they signed."** Two sentences of his, joined. "People have to AGREE to be under surveillance, in an agreement they signed" was said of the grammar that should be in Agreements, before "One leg"; "DATA cMIPs focused on agreements" was said after. That people agree to the data cMIPs in an agreement they signed is the lead's joining. Draft 12 follows it, and lists what it leaves (section 8.2, items 14 to 16).
7. **"That grammar is in Agreements only, never extended to the other layers".** His words: "I think the grammar to support it should be there, in agreements only." A leaning ("I think"), and "it" is the stretch: requests from further upstream. After "One leg", what grammar is left for it is not said (section 8.2, item 16).
8. **Not in the line:** "Why would the service care? Obligation fulfilled on their end."; "The partner cannot trust the receiver, it can only trust the end of the waterfall."; "the answer is it depends, and if it depends it needs to be built to accommodate both"; "upstream of payment can request, and anyone upstream of them should be able to do so too."; "it’s a huge stretch on purpose. To see where it snaps." Draft 12 quotes them in rule 11's reasoning, and writes none of them as a rule.
9. **"the deal with the service is signed by all of them, as the work's Terms require, and by the service"** (Who signs the deal). His words: "The service signed with work", and "the agreement with the service is signed by all". "As the work's Terms require" is the lead's, from his earlier "whatever satisfies the collective agreement". Which agreement "the agreement with the service" is (the work's own deal with the service that divides for it, or something signed with a service that pays it) is not said (section 8.2, item 1).
10. **"The report about a payment comes from its receiver."** His words have "only": "the report about the payment only comes from the receiver." Draft 12 reads "only" as: of the work's people (section 8.2, item 6).
11. **"A request for reports beyond that (surveillance) can only be written into that deal".** His words: "It’s in that agreement that a request for surviliance can be cooked." "Only" is the lead's. Draft 12 writes "Terms they signed" (rule 11.6), which the other exchange supports, and does not say that the deal is the only place.
12. **"A party that is an agreement signs, as its own Terms require; draft 11's 'never signs' gives way"; "is dropped; with it, no collective could sign anything".** His words: "if you keep that quote a collective becomes nearly impossible to build." He does not say what replaces the sentence. "As its own Terms require" is from his earlier words on the counter signature.
13. **"(a small work by all its owners, a collective by a vote or a named delegate, as its rule says)".** "Signed by all" is his. "A vote or a named delegate" is the lead's. Draft 12 writes both, as the brief asks, and marks the second as the lead's (rule 1's reasoning).
14. **Not covered by the exchange at all:** whether every agreement named as a party must sign; what its signing binds its people to; by which acts it is shown. Draft 12 leaves the default of rule 4.1 as it was and lists them (P17).

## E.4 Draft 11's list of open items, unchanged, and what draft 12 does with it

What follows is section 8.2 of `draft-11.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 11" there mean draft 11; rule numbers there are draft 11's.

Each item is where one of draft 11's changes touches something the exchanges "Must every leg report?", "The report, after the click test" and "May and can't" do not cover. Each is left as draft 10 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so. Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the MOR lead's proposal, say more than the exchanges is listed in `history.md` (Part D).

1. **The block is a proposal.** Rule 11 and K8's row now rest on `report-block-1.md`, which is the MOR lead's proposal, not a decision, and is in no spec. If its wording changes, they follow. Everything the block itself leaves open is in section 8.1.
2. **Is sight enough?** In plain words: the click test showed that one silent leg hides everyone beyond it, and they never chose that leg. Draft 11 does not change that. It makes it visible: a payer reads, before paying, which legs called for reports. The ledger's line on "Must every leg report?" says that if a click cannot be followed without every leg reporting, rule 11 changes. The later exchange answers with "may or may not" and "if needed". Whether seeing it before paying is enough for the wish is his. It is written as a cost (section 7).
3. **Where the call sits.** The field `calls`, in Terms, is Claude's: the least that lets the text be read. "The act that holds the task" is read as the Terms that name a cMIP answering K8 (a service's deal). Terms have no field that names a task as such. How a call is written is the block's (section 8.1).
4. **Who makes the call in a deal.** His words: "Who signed the act that holds the task, no?" A deal is between a service and a work as a party, and a party named as an agreement never signs (rule 1.2). So as the rules stand, a deal's call is made by the service and whoever else signed the deal, not by the people of the work, whose only say is which deal their work names as its receiver. Whether that is what is meant is not said (P17).
5. **The doer and the receiver, for K8.** The block leaves the two roles to each task. Draft 11 says: the doer is whoever carried out the passing on; a receiver is whoever something was passed on to. Claude's reading, the least that lets rule 11 be read.
6. **The tie between two legs.** The proposal speaks of the two halves of one call. A click that climbs needs more: the report of the leg that paid and the report of the leg that received answer two different calls, each made by other signers. Draft 11 reads the tie as holding wherever two reports name the same act, whichever call each answers (rule 11.4(b)), because the brief's change 3b asks it ("neither needs to know the other"). The proposal's wording does not yet say so.
7. **What "match" means for K8.** The block leaves it to the task. Draft 11 says: the same amount stated for the same settlement. Whether two honest reports can state different amounts for one settlement (a fee a way of paying takes on the way) is the Money layer's to say (P20).
8. **What lies beyond a silent leg.** Draft 10 answered "not shown" for a leg with no report and for every leg beyond it. The block says such a leg is shown as "unanswered" or "no report called for", and read as nothing else. Draft 11 uses those words for the leg itself, and keeps "not shown" only for the legs beyond, as part of that result: their own reports are still read. The exchanges do not speak of the legs beyond.
9. **Readable without the leg's cMIP.** Kept from draft 10 (rule 11.3). The exchanges do not speak of it. It holds for what K8 adds because that is written in this text. That the block's own three parts are readable without any cMIP is the block's to say.
10. **Kept, and still owed.** K8's row asks for the two apart, which answers what the test found (a held payout that looked like a fee). Not said: whether money shown as still owed is later shown as paid, and by which act; and whether a report that states nothing as kept says "nothing was kept" or says nothing.
11. **Reports that do not agree.** They are shown side by side and neither is chosen. Whether that is a fork in the sense of rule 12, and whether anything ends it, is still not said (it was item 7 of draft 10's list).
12. **Pools, loops, and a person who receives for others.** Left open, each with what the test showed: P6, P8 and P20.
13. **Which sequences use it.** Rule 11 is written for any sequence. Whether the agreements of a baker's sequence (rule 14) call for reports is still not said (P18).
14. **Where a report sits, and rule 6.** Draft 10's leg report was a Statement within the leg's agreement, and so stood in its signer's own line. A report now sits where its call says. Whether it is a Statement of this text, and whether it stands in a signer's line, is not said (section 8.1).
15. **"May and can't."** An observation, not a rule: no MUST was reworded or tested because of it. The block's own MUSTs (what a verifier shows) are named in rule 11.4 as the block's. Rule 11 keeps three MUSTs of its own, all from draft 10: read leg by leg with who signed; show both reports that disagree and choose neither; a leg report readable without the leg's cMIP.
16. **Words.** "Call", "report" and the five shown words are the MOR lead's, as technical wording ("report" and "call for" are also in his own words; "confirmations" is his word too, and is not used). "Leg report" is kept from draft 10. "Kept" and "still owed" are from the brief; "doer" and "receiver" from the proposal. A receiver in rule 11 (one to whom something was passed on) is not the `receiver` of rule 1 (who receives for an agreement), though it is often the same one; if the two words confuse, the wording is the MOR lead's to change.
17. **Words that may be typos.** The head of the ledger now lists "don.t", "watever" and "techincally" as corrected with him. They stand in this text as draft 10 has them, since the brief changes nothing else. The three new exchanges hold others ("wether", "am o", "Don*t"); draft 11 quotes around them.
18. **Not brought in.** The proposal notes that the MICS in force has its own receipts and acknowledgements, and that each lower layer will decide whether its receipt becomes a report. Nothing is written from it here. Task K9 (Delivery), whose answer is an acknowledgment, is left as draft 10 has it. The aside in the ledger on a test in which a grant was refused is still not brought in.
19. **Nothing was tested.** The click sketch was built on draft 10. No test was run against draft 11: whether the block lets a reader follow the same click, and how it reads the sketch's six runs, is for a test to show.

**What draft 12 does with it.** Item 2 (is sight enough?): narrowed by "one leg"; still his (draft 12, section 8.2, item 3). Item 4 (who makes the call in a deal): answered, the work signs its deal; what is left is P17 (item 1 there). Item 6 (the tie between two legs): answered for the usual case, the two reports of a leg answering the same call; the wider reading stays (item 2 there). Item 12: a person who receives for others is answered (rules 11.2, 11.5; P20); pools and loops stand. Item 16: "confirms" is now used, of a receiver's report. Item 19: draft 12 was not tested either. Every other item stands as written.

**Read for draft 13, and nothing else:** `docs/agreements-from-scratch/draft-12.md`, this file and `companion-3.md` (branch `claude/agreements-draft-12`, pull request 165); `docs/decisions.md` and `docs/lead/proposals/report-block-1.md` (branch `claude/lead-records-2`); `docs/click-sketch-12-report.md` (branch `claude/click-sketch`, pull request 163: the Dubsar Suite lead's test of draft 12). Draft 13 is draft 12 with five changes, numbered as its brief numbers them, and nothing else. Three exchanges of the ledger are brought in, each read as a whole: **"An outvoted member and being seen"** (S48), **"The payer's seat"** (S49) and **"The contest"** (S50). Three wiring fixes are the MOR lead's, as technical wording, recorded with "The payer's seat" (L1 to L3). The fifth change is housekeeping (H4). No research text, earlier draft or sketch code was reopened.

Two definitions are added, **buyer** and **contest**; **party** is changed. The answers are unchanged. No parameter is added or removed; P8, P17 and P20 are changed. One cost is new ("A contest reaches one leg") and three are changed ("A sequence is readable only as far as its legs call for reports, and are answered", "One leg, and no further", "Paid, and the author gone").

## F.2 What the second click test showed, and which change answers it

The Dubsar Suite lead built the same click on draft 12 and the report block (`docs/click-sketch-12-report.md`, branch `claude/click-sketch`; twelve runs). In short: the money reaches everyone, and the reports fit together with nothing added; the three flaws of the first test are gone. But draft 12 turned "can it be followed?" into "who can follow it?", and the answer was: each person their own leg, and the payer nothing.

| The test's item | What it showed | What answers it |
|---|---|---|
| 2a, and its question 4 | The payer, the "I" of the wish, signed nothing in the sequence and so sees no leg at all after paying, not even that the payment arrived. | Change 1 (rule 11.7), from "The payer's seat": paying is the buyer's own leg; the buyer receives the receiver's confirmation, then access. Not through a call naming "whoever paid", which the test leaned to: by default. Whether the money reached every member is not shown by default. |
| 2b | "By all its owners" can be read one level, or through every agreement above; the second reading makes a film's deal need every band member's signature. | Change 4, first fix (rule 1.2): one level. The MOR lead's. |
| 2c | A band member who receives for the band keeps her own 40%, and K8's row could only call it "kept (a fee)". | Change 4, second fix (K8's row): a line for the doer's own part. The MOR lead's. |
| 2d | A partner's view of the whole climb was built with the block alone, each leg's call naming the partner's place; a data cMIP would add only who may be named as a reader and how legs are stitched. | Change 4, third fix (rule 11.6(a)): the grammar names no data cMIP for it; a data cMIP adds stitching. The MOR lead's. |
| 3d | Owed money later paid is tied by nothing to its earlier "owed" line. | Not answered; P20 already lists it. |
| 3e, 3f | Pools work if one report may cover many payments; loops matter only to a reader who stitches legs. | Not changed. P6 stands; P8 notes that a loop is the stitching cMIP's. |
| 3g | A person holding for others: answered by draft 12, with 2c above. | Change 4, second fix. |

Changes 2 and 3 answer no item of the test: the contest comes from the same conversation as the payer's seat, and the outvoted member from draft 12's own list (its section 8.2, item 15).

- **Rule 1:** plain words, one new paragraph; 1.2, "One level"; one protection added; one new paragraph of reasoning.
- **Rule 4:** 4.10, three words.
- **Rule 11:** plain words (what a leg report says; a holder's own part; "One leg" rewritten from its fifth sentence on; "The buyer's seat" and "The contest", new; who holds which reports); 11.2, two words; 11.4(e), its last sentence; 11.5, one sentence added; 11.6, rewritten and set out as (a) to (c); 11.7 and 11.8, new; the lie and whom it protects; four new paragraphs of reasoning, and one sentence of the paragraph before them.

## F.4 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines say more than the exchanges

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check". Each item is something a line says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 13 does. The brief repeats most of the lines; where it does, draft 13 follows the exchange, and says so.

1. **"not the grammar's to answer"** (An outvoted member). His words: "That’s up to the cMIP to decide, not me." "Not me" is read by the lead as "not the grammar's". Draft 13 follows it: rule 11.6(b) answers nothing beyond the rule the member signed.
2. **"Which consent a data cMIP asks for is that cMIP's".** He says "the cMIP", and "to decide". That it is a data cMIP, and that what it decides is which consent it asks for, is the lead's, from the question put to him (draft 13, section 8.2, item 17).
3. **"which cMIP and which rule apply is the signers' choice when they make the agreement".** His words: "it’s up to the signers to decide at inception of the agreement." They do not say what the signers decide; "which cMIP and which rule" is the lead's. "At inception" says nothing of a later version (section 8.2, item 16).
4. **"where its rules say the majority decides, the majority decides".** His, nearly word for word. Whether the rule must speak of being seen is not said (section 8.2, item 18).
5. **"The payment to the work is the buyer's own leg"** (The payer's seat). His words: "Leg by leg by default." The payment as a leg of its own is the lead's reading, stated in the question. Draft 13 follows it (rule 11.7; section 8.2, item 1).
6. **"the buyer receives the receiver's confirmation".** His words: "Buyer needs to receive confirmation payment has been received." They do not say from whom. And "needs" is stronger than the line's "receives"; draft 13 makes nobody confirm (section 8.2, items 2 and 3).
7. **"that is further legs, seen only as one leg each, or through a data cMIP people agreed to".** Not in the exchange. His words say only what the buyer is not shown by default: "Not that the money reached all members for example." What lies beyond the default is the lead's, from the earlier exchange "A person who receives for others". Draft 13 writes it, as the brief asks, and marks it (section 8.2, item 6).
8. **Not in the line:** the question he was asked (may a leg's call name "whoever paid"?) is answered neither yes nor no. Draft 13 neither forbids nor writes it.
9. **The three settlements** in that line are the MOR lead's own, as the line says. Nothing in the exchange speaks of them. Draft 13 writes them as the lead's (L1 to L3 above), and says so in the reasoning of rules 1 and 11.
10. **"A contest comes only where what was bought was not delivered after payment"** (The contest). His: "only if goods or service were not delivered after payment." "What was bought" is the lead's shortening.
11. **"It reaches the agreement the buyer bought under".** His words: "it all started from an agreement, so it’s just need to reach back the agreement." "The buyer bought under" is the lead's.
12. **"its signers are contested first, not the services they picked. If those signers believe the fault is their service's, they contest the service in turn."** His, but for "in turn". And before it, he answered "À" to the lead's two readings, and began from "it contests the whole sequence"; the line records only where he ended. "Maybe" is his: "So maybe the whole sequence is not needed". Draft 13 follows the end of the exchange, and lists the leaning (section 8.2, item 8).
13. **"So a contest also goes leg by leg, each answering for its own choices; the whole sequence is not needed."** "Leg by leg" and "each answering for its own choices" are the lead's. The brief asks for "leg by leg"; draft 13 uses it as the name of rule 11.8 and does not write "each answering for its own choices" as a rule. Whether a service contests further, and whether the works a work stands on are contested in the same way, is not in the exchange (section 8.2, item 11).

## F.5 Draft 12's list of open items, unchanged, and what draft 13 does with it

What follows is section 8.2 of `draft-12.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 12" there mean draft 12; rule numbers there are draft 12's.

Draft 12 is draft 11 with three changes: an agreement as a party signs (rules 1.2, 4.4); every leg is the same exchange (rules 11.2, 11.5); a report travels one leg (rule 11.6). They come from the exchanges "The same rule, in the Dubsar Suite lead's window", "A person who receives for others" and "Who signs the deal, and who can call". Each item below is where a change touches something those exchanges do not cover. Each is left as draft 11 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so. Where the ledger's arrived-at lines say more than the exchanges is listed in `history.md` (Part E).

**Three items of draft 11's list that these changes touch: what is answered, and what stays open.**

1. **Who makes the call in a deal** (draft 11's item 4). *Answered:* the work signs its deal, as its own Terms require, and so does the service. The call in a deal is made by both, and is the work's people's as much as the service's (rules 1.2, 11.2). *Stays open:* by which acts the work's signing is shown, and what it binds its people to (P17). And which deal he meant. His words are "The service signed with work". This text writes it for a deal as it defines one: between a service and the work it serves. In the click test's case, the service that pays a band member has its deal with another work, further up, and the band signed nothing with it. Whether the band then signs something with that service too is not said.
2. **The tie between two legs** (draft 11's item 6). *Answered, for the usual case:* at every leg the one who passed on reports and the one who received confirms. Those two reports answer the same call, the leg's own, so the tie is between two halves of one call, as the proposal has it. One leg is linked to the next by the one who received: the receiver of one leg is the doer of the next (rule 11.5). *Stays open:* rule 11.4(b) still ties any two reports that name the same act, whichever call each answers, as draft 11 has it. That now matters only to a reader who holds the reports of two legs, and by rule 11.6 such a reader exists only where both legs agreed. Whether reading across legs should leave the grammar altogether, for data cMIPs, is not said.
3. **Is sight enough?** (draft 11's item 2). *What changes:* before paying, a reader still sees which legs called for reports, and now also which legs agreed to be read further (rule 11.4(e)). After paying, the grammar shows a reader the reports of the legs they signed, and no others: one leg. Whether the click reached the people at the far end is no longer the grammar's to show. It is for data cMIPs, where every leg on the way agreed. His words: "the answer is it depends, and if it depends it needs to be built to accommodate both". *Stays open:* whether that is enough for the wish ("can I with a single click reward all the people") is his. It is written as a cost (section 7, "One leg, and no further").

**An agreement as a party signs.**

4. **Pointed at, or signing.** In plain words: a film that names a song as a party does not need the song's owners to sign; a deal does need the work to sign. The exchange covers the deal only. So the default of rule 4.1 still does not count a party named as an agreement, and Terms that need its signing say so in their own `in_force` rule. Whether the grammar should itself tell the two apart is not said (P17).
5. **How the signing is shown, and what it binds.** Not said: which acts show that a work signed (each owner's assent, one act for a vote, a delegate's signature), and what its signing binds each of its people to (P17). "A vote or a named delegate" is the MOR lead's wording, from the brief.
6. **"the report about the payment only comes from the receiver".** Read as: of the work's people, only its receiver reports on a payment (rule 11.2). It is not read as taking away the service's own report, which he describes in the other exchange ("The service fulfilled its obligation, reported that it did so").
7. **Bound.** Draft 11 said an agreement named as a party is never bound. Draft 12 says that only of one that has not signed. For one that has, nothing is said (P17).

**Every leg is the same exchange.**

8. **May, or must.** His words: "So the same exchange of reports happen within the band." That describes what happens. Draft 12 keeps the earlier "may or may not": a band's own Terms may carry a call, and nothing makes them. If he meant that such a leg must report, rule 11 changes.
9. **Where a work with no service holds the task.** Draft 11 read "the act that holds the task" as Terms that name a cMIP answering K8. A band whose member receives for all may name none. Rule 11.2 now also reads a work as holding the task where its receiver is one of its own parties and others have a part. That is Claude's reading, the least that lets "within the band" be read.
10. **"Automatic on certain rails".** Not said: who signs an automatic report, and whether a settlement can itself be the confirmation. It is the Money layer's (section 8.1; P20). This text says "ways of paying" where he says "rails".
11. **A receiver confirms under a call it did not sign.** A band member paid by a service further up did not sign that service's deal. Their confirmation answers its call all the same, as any receiver's does in draft 11: nobody is made to answer. Left as draft 11 has it.

**One leg.**

12. **What "one leg" covers.** His words: "One leg, that’s all it can do." Rule 11.6 reads three things into it: a call expects reports only on the task of its own Terms; nobody outside a leg can call for its reports; no report carries another leg's reports onward. That is Claude's reading, the least that lets it be checked.
13. **"Not shown", for a reader beyond the leg.** A reader who does not hold a leg's reports cannot honestly say its call is "unanswered". Rule 11.4(d) says "not shown" there. Claude's reading; the shown words are the block's (section 8.1).
14. **Data cMIPs.** "DATA cMIPs focused on agreements" are his words. Not said: what one is, which task of section 5 it answers, and how Terms say their signers agreed to one. No task is added.
15. **Who has agreed.** "People have to AGREE to be under surveillance, in an agreement they signed." For a small work, every owner signed. For a collective that signs by a vote or a delegate, a member who was outvoted signed only the collective's own agreement. Whether that is agreeing is not said. Rule 11.6 says "Terms they signed" and points to rule 5.1.
16. **"the grammar to support it should be there, in agreements only".** He said this before "One leg". Draft 12 reads the two together: the grammar gives one leg; beyond it, the only support in the grammar is what it already has (a cMIP named in signed Terms). If he meant a block of its own for seeing further, it is not written.
17. **"upstream of payment can request, and anyone upstream of them should be able to do so too".** Said inside what he called "a huge stretch on purpose". Draft 12 does not write it as a rule. It is read as something a data cMIP may give, where those seen agreed.
18. **Agreements only, and a block for all of MOR.** The report is the shared language's, so other layers may use one-leg reports. What is Agreements' alone is the way to agree to be read further. The proposal for the block does not yet say "one leg" (section 8.1).

**The rest.**

19. **The Dubsar Suite lead's list.** The first exchange records that the link by a shared act, amounts, and kept-or-owed are the MOR lead's proposals, "not yet his". Draft 11 wrote them because its brief asked. Draft 12 leaves them, and they are still to check.
20. **Words.** "Leg", "obligation", "waterfall", "surveillance", "upstream" and "data cMIPs" are his. "Confirms" is used for a receiver's report, from his "confirmed it via report". "Doer" is the proposal's. "The same exchange" is his.
21. **Words that may be typos.** Quoted as the ledger has them: "surviliance", "else’s", "happen", "become".
22. **Nothing was tested.** No test was run against draft 12, or against draft 11.

**What draft 13 does with it.** Item 1: one level says that the band does not sign the deal of a work further up; whether it signs something with that service is still not said. Item 2 (the tie between two legs): a reader whom several legs' calls name ties them as rule 11.4 says, and a data cMIP adds the stitching; narrowed, not closed. Item 3 (is sight enough?): answered in part by "The payer's seat": the buyer sees their own leg, and by default no more; whether that is enough for the wish is still his (draft 13, section 8.2, item 7). Item 8 (may, or must): stands, and the buyer's "needs to receive" joins it. Item 11: stands, and now matters more, since a call may name a reader from outside. Item 12: rule 11.6 now also says that "where" may be a place outside the leg. Item 14 (data cMIPs): narrowed: the grammar names none, and one adds only stitching; what it is, and which task it answers, is still not said. Item 15 (who has agreed): answered by "An outvoted member and being seen" (rule 11.6(b)). Item 16: the MOR lead's third fix settles that no block is written for seeing further. Item 18: sharpened into a tension draft 13 does not settle (its section 8.2, item 21). Item 20: "buyer", "seat", "contest", "stitching" and "one level" are added (its item 24). Item 21: four more (its item 25). Item 22: draft 12 was tested, by the second click test (F.2); draft 13 was not. Every other item stands as written.

# Part G. Draft 14

**Read for draft 14, and nothing else:** `docs/agreements-from-scratch/draft-13.md`, this file and `companion-3.md` (branch `claude/agreements-draft-13-69eha3`, pull request 166); `docs/decisions.md` and `docs/lead/proposals/report-block-2.md` (branch `claude/lead-records-2`). Draft 14 is draft 13 with four changes, numbered as its brief numbers them, and nothing else. One exchange of the ledger is brought in, read as a whole: **"Reports are data"** (S51). The proposal `report-block-2.md` is the MOR lead's, written from that exchange; it replaces `report-block-1.md` as what the draft points to. No research text, earlier draft, test report or sketch code was reopened.

## G.1 Changes, and the words each comes from

| | The exchange | What changed (Claude's reading) |
|---|---|---|
| S51, first part | **Reports are data** (draft 13, pull request 166, its section 8.2, item 21). The MOR lead named a tension: the report block is for all of MOR, and seeing further was to be in Agreements only; he leaned that in every layer a call sends reports only between the two sides of its own leg, naming outside readers being Agreements' alone. He: "Leg by leg only." Later in the same exchange the MOR lead said the report proposal would lose its "where". He: "Well we had agreed the where was needed, we just did not realize the where was implied." | Draft 14, **change 1: leg by leg only.** A report moves between the two sides of its own leg, and no further, in every layer. A call says whether a report is wanted and from whom; it never names a place or a reader. The where is implied by the leg: a report goes to the other side of its own leg, where that side receives (rules 11.2, 11.6). Removed: every place where draft 13 let a call say where its reports are expected, or let that be a place of someone outside the leg (G.2). |
| S51, second part | The MOR lead asked whether that holds in Agreements too, withdrawing his fix in which each leg's call names a partner. He: "This to me this is data. Data needs to move along paths that people agree, with signature." | Draft 14, **change 2: reports are data.** Rule 11.6(a) is rewritten: a report is data; any path by which it reaches someone who is not a side of its leg (a partner seeing further, a request from upstream) is a path the people whose data it carries agreed to, with their signature, built as a data cMIP that Terms they signed name. The grammar gives no such path itself. **Withdrawn:** draft 13's rule 11.6(a), the partner's view by calls alone (draft 13's L3: the MOR lead's fix, not the words of Nobody, allegedly), and with it the sentence that a data cMIP adds only stitching. **Kept:** rule 11.6(b), the outvoted member, unchanged but for one phrase ("by a call in those Terms that names that place" is now "by naming such a data cMIP"); rule 11.6(c), reworded, since one leg is now the block's and not only this text's. |
| O3 | **Draft 13's open items these touch.** Not his words: the brief's third change. | Draft 14, **change 3:** its section 8.2 says, for items 21, 22, 23, 6, 7, 16, 26 and 27 of draft 13's list, what changes 1 and 2 answer and what stays open; then what each change leaves open of its own. Draft 13's list is kept unchanged in G.4. |

This Part G holds the one table of its changes (this one), where each change landed (G.2), where the ledger's lines and the proposal say more than the exchange (G.3), and draft 13's list of open items, unchanged, with what draft 14 does with it (G.4). `draft-13.md` stays in the repository as the record pull request 166 points to. Wherever draft 13 pointed to `report-block-1.md`, draft 14 points to `report-block-2.md`. `companion-3.md` is changed in one row and two sentences. |

Draft 13 had fourteen grammar rules and thirteen tasks; so has draft 14. No rule, clause or task is added or removed, and no number moves. No field is added or removed: `calls` stays, and no longer holds a place. No definition is added; **leg**, and **report** and **call**, are changed. The answers are unchanged. No parameter is added or removed; P8 is changed in one sentence. No cost is added; two are changed ("A sequence is readable only as far as its legs call for reports, and are answered", "One leg, and no further").

## G.2 Where each change landed

Every place where draft 13 let a call say where its reports go, or name an outside reader, and what draft 14 has there:

- **Head:** the paragraph "And one thing here is this text's alone" is now "Reports are data". The pointer to the proposal names `report-block-2.md`.
- **Section 0:** rule 11 in plain words (the call "says from whom and where"; "it goes where its own leg's call says"; the partner named by a leg's call; "in Agreements only"), and the last paragraph (who reads the climb).
- **Section 2:** **leg** (its two sides, for a report); **report** and **call** (no "where"; the other side of its own leg; a report is data).
- **Section 3.1:** `calls` (no "where each report is expected").
- **Rule 1:** the last sentence of its reasoning ("who sees what").
- **Rule 11, plain words:** the call (no where, no reader); who sees which calls were answered; "One leg", rewritten; a new paragraph, "Reports are data", which takes what draft 13's "One leg" said of the partner, the outvoted member and the other layers; "The buyer's seat", three sentences; the paragraph on following a click both ways (who holds which reports, and no "where to look").
- **Rule 11, precisely:** 11.2 (the call names no place and no reader); 11.4(a) (where to look: with the two sides); 11.4(d) (who may say "unanswered"); 11.4(e) (before paying: from whom, and whether Terms name a data cMIP); 11.6, rewritten, (a) to (c); 11.7, its last sentences. 11.1, 11.3, 11.5 and 11.8 are unchanged.
- **Rule 11, the rest:** two protections; in the reasoning, "where the next report is", the end of "One leg", one sentence of "The buyer's seat", a new paragraph "Leg by leg only; reports are data", and the paragraph on the MOR lead's two fixes, which is now about one ("A holder's own part").
- **Section 5.1:** the paragraph on seeing beyond one leg.
- **Section 6:** P8, its last sentence.
- **Section 7:** the two costs named above.
- **Section 8:** 8.1, the report block's item and the item beside it; 8.2, new; 8.3, brought up to date.

Rules 2 to 10 and 12 to 14, section 1, section 3.2, the tasks' table and every other parameter and cost are unchanged.

## G.3 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the proposal, say more than the exchange

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check"; `report-block-2.md` is his proposal. Each item is something a line, or the proposal, says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 14 does. Item numbers in brackets are draft 14's section 8.2.

1. **"in the core, in every layer"**. His words: "Leg by leg only." "In every layer" is from the MOR lead's question, which he answered; "in the core" is the lead's. Draft 14 follows both.
2. **"between the doer and the receiver of its own leg"**. His words name no sides. "The two sides of its own leg" is the lead's, from his question; "doer" and "receiver" are the proposal's words. Draft 14 uses them as rule 11.2 already does, and lists what they leave unsaid (item 9).
3. **"A report is data"**. His words: "This to me this is data." What "this" is, is the lead's reading (item 15).
4. **"data moves only along paths people agreed to"**. His words: "Data needs to move along paths that people agree, with signature." "Only" is the lead's (item 18). Whose agreement is not in his words (item 16).
5. **"built as a data cMIP"**. Not in this exchange. From the earlier one, "A person who receives for others": "For everything else’s it’s DATA cMIPs focused on agreements". The brief asks for it; draft 14 writes it and marks it (item 17).
6. **"(a partner seeing further, a request from upstream)"**. The examples are from the earlier exchange too, where the second was said inside "a huge stretch on purpose" (item 19).
7. **"never by a call in the core naming an outside reader"**. Not his words. He was asked whether leg by leg holds in Agreements too, and answered with the principle about data, not with yes or no. A call sits in signed Terms, so a call naming a partner is something its signers agreed to, with signature; his words do not say it cannot be. The withdrawal is the lead's own, made in his question. The brief asks for it; draft 14 follows, and lists it (item 12).
8. **"The MOR lead's fix … is withdrawn"**. So it is, by the lead. His words neither ask for it nor object.
9. **"the "where" stays, and is implied by the leg"**. His words: "the where was implied". By what is the lead's (item 10).
10. **"a report goes to the other side of its own leg, where that side receives"**. The lead's. How "where that side receives" is found is open in the proposal itself.
11. **"no call needs to name a place"**. The line says "needs"; the proposal (R1, R3) and the brief say a call does not, and cannot name another reader. His words say only that the where was implied. Draft 14 follows the brief: `calls` holds no place (item 11).
12. **Not in the lines: "by default".** On "The payer's seat" he said "Leg by leg by default."; here, "Leg by leg only." The lines do not say how the two sit together. Draft 14 reads them as one train of thought (item 8).
13. **Not in the lines: "in agreements only".** His earlier words, "the grammar to support it should be there, in agreements only. Never extended to the other layers", are neither repeated nor withdrawn here. The lead's question said that naming outside readers would be "Agreements' alone", and the answer was "Leg by leg only." Draft 14 keeps the least (rule 11.6(c); items 1 and 21).
14. **The proposal: "two sides who both signed".** His words do not say who the sides are, or that both signed. In draft 14 a receiver may confirm under a call in Terms it did not sign, as in draft 13 (items 3 and 9).
15. **The proposal: "The one path the core gives is the leg".** The lead's framing of the two sentences together. Draft 14 uses it in the head and in rule 11's plain words.
16. **The proposal: "everyone further along or further up, whose data moves nowhere they did not sign for".** The lead's. Draft 14 writes the like in rule 11's protection, for reports only (item 20).

## G.4 Draft 13's list of open items, unchanged, and what draft 14 does with it

What follows is section 8.2 of `draft-13.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 13" there mean draft 13; rule numbers there are draft 13's. Its rule 11.6(a) is the clause draft 14 rewrote.

Draft 13 is draft 12 with four changes: the buyer's seat (rule 11.7); the contest, leg by leg (rule 11.8); an outvoted member (rule 11.6(b)); and three wiring fixes of the MOR lead's (rule 1.2, K8's row, rule 11.6(a)). The first three come from the exchanges "The payer's seat", "The contest" and "An outvoted member and being seen". The fixes are the MOR lead's, as technical wording, recorded with "The payer's seat"; they are not the words of Nobody, allegedly. Each item below is where a change touches something the exchanges do not cover. Each is left as draft 12 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so. Where the ledger's arrived-at lines say more than the exchanges is listed in `history.md` (Part F, section F.4).

**The buyer's seat.**

1. **Whose leg.** His words: "Leg by leg by default." That the payment to the work is a leg of its own, the buyer's, is the MOR lead's reading. Draft 13 follows it (rule 11.7), because it is the least that gives the buyer a confirmation without making the buyer a signer of any leg.
2. **May, or must.** His words: "Buyer needs to receive confirmation payment has been received." Draft 13 makes nobody confirm, as draft 12 makes nobody report: a payment nobody confirmed is shown as one side only. If he meant that whoever receives must confirm, rule 11.7 changes. This joins draft 12's item 8, which is still open.
3. **Who confirms.** His words do not say who. "The receiver's confirmation" is the MOR lead's. Draft 13 reads it as the receiver now of the agreement paid (rule 1.4), as rule 11.2 reads a receiver. Where a work's receiver is its deal with a service, who under that deal confirms to the buyer (the service, or one of the work's people) is not said.
4. **With no call written.** In the report block, a report answers a call, and a call is made by signers. "By default" is read as: for the buyer's own leg, the confirmation is expected without any call. That is Claude's reading, and so is using the block's words (matched, mismatched, one side only) for it. Whether the block has such a default is the MOR lead's to write (section 8.1).
5. **Access.** "Then, access to wathever they bought." Not said: what access is, who gives it, whether it must wait for the confirmation, and what shows that it was given. The grammar does not read it (rule 11.7; section 8.1).
6. **Beyond the default.** His words say what the buyer is not shown: "Not that the money reached all members for example." They do not say what lies beyond the default. That it is a further leg's own call, one leg each, or a data cMIP people agreed to, is the MOR lead's. The question he was asked (may a leg's call name "whoever paid"?) is not answered yes or no in his words. Draft 13 neither forbids it nor writes it: a call may name any place (rule 11.6(a)); how "whoever paid" would be written as a place is the block's.
7. **Is that enough for the wish?** After paying, the buyer knows the payment arrived, and has what they bought. Whether "can I with a single click reward all the people" asks for more sight than that is his. It is written as a cost (section 7, "One leg, and no further").

**The contest.**

8. **From the whole sequence to the agreement.** He began: "it contests the whole sequence". He ended: "So maybe the whole sequence is not needed, the signers of the agreement are contested at first". Draft 13 follows the end, reading the exchange as a whole. "Maybe" is his: a leaning.
9. **What a contest is.** No kind, field or answer is defined for it: it is a Statement of a kind a cMIP gives (K11). That it is made within the agreement the buyer bought under, and names the payment, is Claude's reading of "it’s just need to reach back the agreement". Whether a buyer who is no party of that agreement can make a Statement within it is as draft 12 has it: not said.
10. **Who is contested "at first".** "The signers of the agreement" is read one level (rule 1.2). Where that agreement signs by a vote or a delegate, who answers for it is not said.
11. **The service, and further.** His words: "If the signer believe the mistake is at the service they contest the service". That this contest is made within the deal they signed with the service is Claude's reading. Not said: whether the service can contest in turn; whether a work's signers can contest the works it stands on, which are legs but not services; and whether the buyer learns of any of it. "Leg by leg", "in turn" and "each answering for its own choices" are the MOR lead's words; the last is not written as a rule.
12. **"Only".** "The contest comes only if goods or service were not delivered after payment." Draft 13 reads a buyer's contest only there. Other disputes are left as draft 12 has them: a member not paid their part, two reports that disagree (shown side by side), something delivered but not as promised (a judge, K10). Whether his "only" also rules those out as contests is not said.
13. **What a contest does.** Nothing here beyond standing on the record: who answers it, how, whether it ever closes, and how money comes back are not the grammar's (escrow, section 5.1; cost "A contest reaches one leg").
14. **Who says it was not delivered.** The buyer, by their own word (rule 8). What would show delivery is the Deliver layer's (K9; section 8.1).
15. **What changed where draft 12 already had contests.** Two places. Section 5.1 said that how a contest reaches an escrow service is that service's cMIP's; it now says the contest reaches the agreement's signers first, and only then is the rest the cMIP's. The cost "Paid, and the author gone" now says whom the contest reaches. Nothing else about contests is changed.

**An outvoted member.**

16. **"At inception".** His words: "it’s up to the signers to decide at inception of the agreement." Draft 13 writes "when they made the agreement". Not said: a later version that names another cMIP, or adds a call naming a reader from outside, made under a power the first Terms gave. Draft 13 reads it as rule 5 reads any power, which is Claude's reading.
17. **What is decided, and by whom.** He says "the cMIP" and "to decide". That it is a data cMIP, and that what it decides is which consent it asks for, is the MOR lead's reading. His "not me" is read by the MOR lead as "not the grammar's".
18. **How wide the rule is.** "If the rules of the agreement state « majority decides » then majority decides." Not said: whether a general rule of that kind is enough, or the rule must speak of being seen. Draft 13 reads the rule as it is written (K4).

**Three wiring fixes, the MOR lead's.**

19. **One level.** Rule 1.2 counts as an agreement's signers the parties its Terms name as signers. Whether someone who fills a role is one of them, and how Terms "say otherwise", is not said (P17).
20. **A holder's own part.** The test offered two ways: a line for the holder's own part, or that part shown as passed on to the holder. The MOR lead took the first. Draft 13 writes it for any doer to whom the leg's Terms give a part. Not said: which act such a part names, since nothing moves when a holder keeps their share (P20).
21. **A reader from outside.** Rule 11.6(a) lets a call's "where" name a place of someone who signed nothing in the leg. The block's "where" is not yet written (section 8.1). And a tension, which draft 13 does not settle: the block is for all of MOR, and "seeing further" was to be in Agreements only. If a call in any layer may name an outside reader, what is Agreements' alone is only the data cMIPs that stitch legs.
22. **What a data cMIP adds.** The test's finding names two things: who may be named as a reader, and how a reader stitches legs. The MOR lead's settlement names one: stitching. Draft 13 writes the lead's. What such a cMIP is, and which task it answers, is still not said.
23. **A receiver who confirms.** Someone paid by a leg they did not sign confirms under its call, and their confirmation now goes wherever that call says, a partner's place included. They agreed to that only by choosing to answer. Left as draft 12 has it (its item 11).

**The rest.**

24. **Words.** "Buyer" is his, and is used for the one who pays for something; "payer" stays wherever draft 12 has it. "Seat" is the Dubsar Suite lead's and the MOR lead's. "Contest" is his. "Partner" is his, from the earlier exchange. "Stitching" and "one level" are the two leads'. "The doer's own part" is Claude's wording for the lead's "a holder's own part".
25. **Words that may be typos.** Quoted as the ledger has them: "wathever", "À", "it’s just need", "the signer believe".
26. **Not brought in from the second test.** That owed money later paid is tied by nothing to its earlier "owed" line (its 3d; P20). That pools work if one report may cover many payments (its 3e; P6). Its proposal to have the partner's view attacked by a leg that reports falsely.
27. **Nothing was tested.** The second click test was built on draft 12. No test was run against draft 13: whether a buyer's confirmation, a contest and a partner named by every leg read as written is for a test to show.

**What draft 14 does with it.** Item 6 (beyond the buyer's default): a call names no reader, so the buyer is not named by one; beyond their own leg, only a path people signed for. Its last sentence ("a call may name any place") no longer holds. Item 7 (enough for the wish?): stands, and is sharper, since no way to see further is left in the block (draft 14, section 8.2, item 5). Item 16 ("at inception"): the case of a later call naming a reader from outside falls away; the case of a later data cMIP stands. Item 21 (a reader from outside): answered by "Reports are data": no call, in any layer, names a place or an outside reader; whether a data cMIP is Agreements' alone stays open. Item 22 (what a data cMIP adds): the lead's settlement, "stitching only", went with the fix it belonged to; a data cMIP is now the path itself; what it is, and which task it answers, is still not said. Item 23 (a receiver who confirms): a confirmation no longer reaches a partner's place; that someone confirms under a call they did not sign stands. Item 24 (words): "stitching" is no longer a word of the text. Item 26: the test's proposal to attack the partner's view falls with that view. Item 27: no test was run against draft 14 either. Items 1 to 5, 8 to 15, 17 to 20 and 25 stand as written.
