# Part J. Draft 17

**Read for draft 17, and nothing else:** `docs/agreements-from-scratch/draft-16.md`, `reasons-16.md`, this file and `companion-3.md` (branch `claude/agreements-draft-16`, pull request 170); `docs/decisions.md` (branch `claude/lead-records-2`). Draft 17 is draft 16 with one change, and nothing else. One exchange of the ledger is brought in, read as a whole: **"The update condition"**. Three earlier exchanges that the change rests on were read again in the ledger: "What the grammar is for", "The condition is a task" and "The wrong cMIP". No research text, spec, earlier draft, review, test report, proposal or sketch code was opened.

**The test for the change**, in the words of Nobody, allegedly: "What the previous version of agreement focused on solving issues… this one supports and specifies the language for creating solutions".

## J.1 The change, and where it comes from

| | Where it comes from | What changed (Claude's reading) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The update condition.** The MOR lead asked whether the condition should have its own task. He: "Condition is short for update condition". To the reading that it is an update signed in advance that triggers by itself: "No, it is wether it is valid or not valid." To the MOR lead's summary (the condition is what an update must meet to count; given the version in force and a proposed update, it answers valid or not valid; it is the same block as the power's rule; rights stop or an offer closes only because a valid update says so; "triggered" and "no longer applies" go): "Yes, and the cMIPs replies." To the reading that an offer closing on a date needs someone to publish the closing update: "No, the cMIP can run the verification via bytes, so it should be able to run." To the reading that the condition is asked of any act, a new claim included: "It answers wether it can be updated or not. Because agreements make these conditions potentially complex the core still needs a simple field the cMIP can then specify in details." | **One block, in one place: G4.11.** Terms may name, in the one field `condition`, their update condition: a cMIP (a rule, task K4) with its parameters. Given the version in force and a proposed update, it answers valid or not valid. It runs from the bytes, and its answer waits for no one to publish. An update it answers not valid replaces nothing, whoever signed it (G4.8). **What is left of the power's rule (G5.2):** who may sign an update. **What is left of the condition (G10.7):** the field, which names the update condition; that a filling stays a filling; that a later version may offer again. **Dropped:** the answers **triggered** and **no longer applies**; everything G10.7 said about what a triggering stops. **Rights (G10.6):** a later version changes a filling's rights only with the holder's assent, or where the update condition of the version it was earned under answers that later version valid. **New identities (G10.5, K12):** as draft 16 has it, less the reading of triggering. |
| 2 | The brief's, as housekeeping. | **The files.** The rules are in `draft-17.md`; each rule's plain words and reasoning are in `reasons-17.md`, which is `reasons-16.md` changed only under G4, G5 and G10 and in two sentences of its opening; this part is added here; `companion-3.md` is changed in four rows, one sentence and its head. `draft-16.md` and `reasons-16.md` stay in the repository. |

Draft 16 had fourteen grammar rules and thirteen tasks; so has draft 17. No grammar rule or task is added or removed, and no task number changes: the merge leaves no task with nothing to do. K4 (Rule) still answers every rule people choose, the update condition among them; K12 (Spot) loses only its reading of triggering. Clauses: G4.11 is new; G4.8 gains one condition; G5.2 loses one sentence and says what is left; G10.5 loses its reading of triggering; G10.6 gains a second way a later version changes a filling's rights; G10.7 is rewritten under the same number. No field is added, removed or renamed: `condition` keeps its name and now holds the update condition. Definitions added: proposed update (under "update"), valid and not valid. Definitions changed: condition, apply. "Triggered" is no longer a term. Answers: **valid** and **not valid** replace **triggered**. Parameters changed: P15, P16 (one reference), P19 (one sentence added), P21. Costs changed: "Triggered is as sure as its condition's cMIP" (now "Valid is as sure as the update condition's cMIP", rewritten), "No default way out" (one clause).

## J.2 Every word that changed

| Draft 16 | Draft 17 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| "condition": what a version states, signed in advance, as changing what applies under it | "condition": short for "update condition" | his words: "Condition is short for update condition" |
| the answer **triggered**, said of a condition | the answers **valid** and **not valid**, said of a proposed update | "No, it is wether it is valid or not valid." |
| "triggered", a defined term | not a term | the MOR lead's summary, to which he said: "Yes, and the cMIPs replies." |
| "no longer applies", plain words for what a triggering may stop | plain words still; rights stop applying only through an update | the same summary |
| the rule of a power for replacement "answers allowed or not" | the update condition answers valid or not valid; the power's rule says who may sign | the same summary ("the same block as the power's rule"); the split is the brief's |
| "the proposed version" | "the proposed update" | the MOR lead's words in the exchange |
| "a condition signed in advance" (G10.5), of an offer that stops taking new people | "what the version in force itself states in advance (a date, a count)" | "condition" now means one thing |
| G10.7's title, "The condition: triggered or not" | "What is left of the condition" | the brief |
| section 5.1's row "A condition" | "The update condition" | his words |
| the cost "Triggered is as sure as its condition's cMIP" | "Valid is as sure as the update condition's cMIP" | as above |

New words: **update condition**, **valid**, **not valid** (his); **proposed update** (the MOR lead's).

## J.3 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchange

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check". There are two; the last replaces the first where they differ. The MOR lead's two withdrawn readings (that a closing needs someone to publish an update; that the condition is asked of any act, a new claim included) are not used. Each item is something a line, or the brief, says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 17 does. Item numbers in brackets are draft 17's section 8.2, unless section 8.3 is named.

**The first line.**

1. **"one block, the update condition"**. "Block" is the MOR lead's. His words: "Condition is short for update condition".
2. **"given the version in force and a proposed update, it answers valid or not valid"**. The first half is the MOR lead's, from his summary. His own: "it is wether it is valid or not valid"; and to the summary, "Yes, and the cMIPs replies." Draft 17 follows the summary he said yes to (item 11).
3. **"and its cMIP gives that answer"**. His words: "and the cMIPs replies". Plural; draft 17 has one cMIP in one field (P21).
4. **"The power's rule (G5.2) and the condition (G10.7) are this one block."** The MOR lead's, from "the same block as the power's rule" in his summary. His words in the earlier exchange "What the grammar is for" gave one cMIP both halves: "the cMiP builds a 2 out of 3 signature scheme", and "allowed to edit this task or not" (item 12).
5. **"Nothing changes by itself"**. The MOR lead's. His words refuse an update "that triggers by itself" ("No, it is wether it is valid or not valid."). Draft 17 writes it of what a version offers and of earned rights (G4.11(f)), and not of whether a spot is still open under the version's own words (section 8.3, item 1).
6. **"or an offer stops taking new people, only through an update the condition answers valid"**. Put to him afterwards as a closing on a date, this was answered "No". The last line withdraws the reading that a closing needs someone to publish. Draft 17 leaves the spot's taking of new identities as draft 16 has it (item 13; section 8.3, item 1).
7. **"The answers 'triggered' and 'no longer applies' are dropped."** The MOR lead's, in the summary he said yes to. "Triggered" was his own word in the exchange "The condition is a task" (item 14).

**The last line.**

8. **"answers whether the agreement can be updated or not"**. His words: "It answers wether it can be updated or not." "The agreement" is the MOR lead's reading of the second "it" (item 17).
9. **"The cMIP runs that check from the bytes, so nothing waits for anyone to publish."** His words: "the cMIP can run the verification via bytes, so it should be able to run." "Nothing waits for anyone to publish" is the MOR lead's. What "it" is, in "it should be able to run", is not said: the cMIP, or the closing the MOR lead had asked about (items 5 and 15).
10. **"The core keeps one simple field"**. His words: "the core still needs a simple field". "One" is the MOR lead's and the brief's (item 16).
11. **"which the cMIP it names specifies in detail, because agreements can make these conditions complex"**. His words: "Because agreements make these conditions potentially complex the core still needs a simple field the cMIP can then specify in details." "These conditions" is plural, in his words.
12. **"replacing the line above where they differ"**. The lines do not say where they differ. Draft 17 reads the difference as item 6 above, and nothing else: the rest of the first line stands.

**The brief.**

13. **"with its parameters"**. The brief's; draft 16's field already carried them (item 18).
14. **"Make them one, in one place"**. The exchange names no place. Draft 17 chooses G4.11 (item 1).
15. **"Who may sign an update (powers, G5) is not changed; only the answer about the update itself becomes the update condition's."** The brief's line between the two halves. The exchange says "the same block" and draws no such line (item 12).
16. **"Rights stop applying, or an offer changes, only through an update the condition answers valid."** "An offer changes" is the brief's, where the first line says "stops taking new people". Draft 17 follows the brief. That a valid update reaches a filling whose holder did not sign it, and under which version's condition, is Claude's reading (items 3 and 4).
17. **"Whether a spot still takes new identities (G10.5, K12) is not covered by the exchange."** The brief's own finding; draft 17 follows it (section 8.3, item 1).
18. **"Keep the task numbers as they are, unless the merge leaves a task with nothing to do."** His answer to the question of a task of its own was "Condition is short for update condition". No task is left empty, and none is added (item 9).

## J.4 Where the change landed

- **Head:** the file names, and Part J.
- **Section 2:** update (a proposed update); condition; valid and not valid (new); triggered (no longer a term); apply; the answers (valid and not valid, in place of triggered).
- **Section 3:** the row `condition`.
- **G4.8:** a replacement also needs the update condition's answer, where one is named. **G4.11:** new. **G4's lie and whom it protects:** one clause each.
- **G5.2:** the power's rule says who may sign; what is left of it.
- **G10.5:** no reading of triggering; "what the version in force itself states in advance"; the update condition is not asked.
- **G10.6:** the second way, (b); what a verifier names with a filling.
- **G10.7:** rewritten: what is left of the condition.
- **Section 5:** K4's and K12's rows. **5.1:** the row "The update condition"; one sentence of the last paragraph. **5.2:** two rows (a share rewritten; a party who wants to leave).
- **Section 6:** P15, P16, P19, P21.
- **Section 7:** "Valid is as sure as the update condition's cMIP"; one clause of "No default way out".
- **Section 8.1:** the list of answers.
- **Section 8.2:** rewritten: what is left of each, the choices draft 17 made, and where the lines and the brief say more than the exchange.
- **Section 8.3:** rewritten: what is left as draft 16 has it.
- **Section 8.4:** a list "From draft 16's list" added; two older lines noted.
- **`reasons-17.md`:** the head; two sentences of the opening; G4 (one paragraph of the plain words, one clause each of the lie and of whom it protects, and a new passage "The update condition"); G5 (one paragraph of the plain words, and a new passage "What is left of it"); G10 (two sentences and one paragraph of the plain words, one clause each of the lie and of whom it protects, and a new passage "The update condition"). Each is marked "(draft 17)".
- **`companion-3.md`:** the heading, one note, one sentence of section 1, and rows #28, #31, #47 and #48.

Not changed, though the change comes near them, and listed in draft 17's section 8.3: a delegation's signed conditions (G5.5); a power to resolve, and all of G12; the `in_force` rule (G4.1).

## J.5 Draft 16's lists of open items, unchanged, and what draft 17 does with them

What follows the notes below is sections 8.2 and 8.3 of `draft-16.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 16" there mean draft 16; "condition", "triggered" and "no longer applies" there have draft 16's meanings (J.2).

What draft 17 does with them:

- **8.2, item 1** (the answer "no longer applies" is not kept): still not kept. The answer that replaced it, **triggered**, is dropped too. The cost named there (a reader who lacks the cMIP is told that the condition triggered, and not what it means) is gone with it; the new cost is in section 7, "Valid is as sure as the update condition's cMIP".
- **8.2, item 2** (is the condition a task with a number of its own): asked again by the MOR lead. His answer, "Condition is short for update condition", says what the condition is and not whether it has a number. No task is added (draft 17's section 8.2, item 9).
- **8.2, item 13** (words): "triggered" is no longer a term.
- **8.2, items 16, 17 and 18** (where the lines said more on the condition): they were about "triggered or not", and are overtaken by the exchange "The update condition".
- **8.3, item 5** (one condition for each version, and which clock it reads): still open, now of the update condition (P21, P16).
- **8.3, item 6** (a later version that offers again): kept (G10.7). The second half (a verifier never answers "triggered" for an act that never counted) is gone with the answer.
- **8.3, item 7** (what a verifier shows of what stops): gone with the answer. What a verifier shows of a change to a filling's rights is draft 17's section 8.3, item 7.
- **8.3, item 15** (nothing was tested): no test was run against draft 17 either.
- **Every other item** stands as written. They are summed up in draft 17's section 8.4.

**8.2 For Nobody, allegedly**

Draft 16 is draft 15 with five changes (`history.md`, Part I). Three exchanges are brought in, each read as a whole: "The contest is an act", "The condition is a task" and "A fork, from MOR's side". The first two answer the two questions draft 15 asked here. The third replaces draft 15's proposal on what a fork removes and on two joins, which is not taken.

**Choices this draft had to make.** Each is Claude's reading, for the MOR lead and for Nobody, allegedly, to take, change or refuse.

1. **The answer "no longer applies" is not kept.** In plain words: draft 15 let a verifier say of a licence "no longer applies". That said what the condition stops, which is now the cMIP's. His words: "triggered or not is what the core cares about." So the verifier's answer is **triggered**, said of the condition, with what triggered it. "No longer applies" stays as plain words (his own were "does not apply anymore"), and is no longer an answer. *What it costs:* a reader who lacks the condition's cMIP is told that the condition triggered, and not what that means for what they hold.
2. **Is the condition a task with a number of its own?** His words: "Condition is the right task". Draft 16 keeps it where draft 15 had it: a cMIP that answers K4 (Rule), named in the field `condition`, whose answer for a condition is triggered or not (K4's row). No task is added; there are still thirteen. If "the right task" means a task of its own in the table, that is one row to add, and it is not added here.
3. **Vow.** The text had no such word. The definition is his words and nothing more: "the same thing but without bytes attached". What "bytes attached" means is not said. One reading, which is not in the text: the bytes are those of a baked work, so that an offer is for a work that has an envelope (G14), and a vow is a promise of something that has none (a service, a deed). It is not written because no grammar rule needs to tell an offer from a vow: a contest reaches the signers of either.
4. **Offer.** His words: "an agreement with unknown participant at the time of signing". Read as: an agreement whose Terms name a party by role. So a contest made against Terms whose every party was known when they were signed is not read by G11.8, and what such a contest is, is not said.
5. **"Contest is MOR act though. It is just extended."** Draft 16 reads "MOR act" as: an act of the grammar, with a kind of its own (`contest`), beside the assent and the counter signature. It does not know what "extended" points at. If the core already has a contest act, the kind is the core's and this text only extends it (section 8.1).
6. **Who contests, and when.** His words are "I contest an offer… or I contest a vow": they do not say who "I" is. The earlier exchange "The contest" is about a buyer: "The contest comes only if goods or service were not delivered after payment." Draft 16 keeps exactly that for a buyer, and says nothing of a contest by anyone else, or for any other cause.
7. **A fork in one signer's own line.** His words are about updates that needed several signatures. G12 is one grammar rule for every line, so draft 16 reads "both tips stop" for one signer's own acts as well. Then an assent that is in a branch is not counted while the fork stands, and its signer is the one who resolves it. Draft 15's proposal said the opposite (both bind), and it is not taken. What is left open is in P9.
8. **Merging is not kept.** Until draft 15 a join could also merge the branches into one. His words: "It is resolved by making one of the two void with an update." So a fork is resolved by continuing one tip, and an act that merges resolves nothing (G12.3). Those who can sign an update can still write a version that takes from both, after resolving: as the next update of the tip that continues.
9. **Which Terms say how a resolving update is signed.** "Signed as an update must be" is read as: as an update of the shared act must be signed, since the shared version is the one in force and neither tip is (G12.3). Draft 15's join was signed the same way.
10. **"Should not", written as MUST NOT.** His words: "A client that sees both tips should not show it as a resolved act". This text uses no SHOULD, so the client's duty is written MUST NOT (G12.8). "It" is read as: either tip, and anything built on either.
11. **A payment made under a tip.** "Nothing built on either counts" meets G13, by which a payer has paid as the version they named says. Draft 16 reads the two together: while the fork stands, the payment is shown with the fork and is not counted under that tip's version; once the fork is resolved, it counts (the tip continues) or stands, shown as disputed (the tip is set aside, G12.5). Meanwhile whoever divides reads the last shared version (G4.7).
12. **Two resolving updates that continue the same tip** are not rivals: each resolves the fork (G12.3), and G12.7 speaks only of two that continue different tips.
13. **Words.** "Tip", "update", "resolved" and "triggered" are his. "Resolving update", "set aside", "built on" and the field name `resolves` are Claude's, as technical wording, for the MOR lead to change. "Never counted as the next version" is the MOR lead's wording, to which he said: "Yes pls".

**Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchanges.** The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's readings, marked "to check". Draft 16 follows the exchange where the two differ. The full list is in `history.md` (section I.3); the ones that change what a rule says:

14. **"as an act, not as a policy: a block".** His words: "Contest is MOR act though. It is just extended." "Block" and "not as a policy" are the MOR lead's. Draft 16 writes the act (G11.8), and adds no policy to what draft 15 had.
15. **"Whom it reaches follows from what it is made against: the signers of that offer or vow."** Not in his words in this exchange, which name what is contested and not whom it reaches. "The signers of the agreement are contested at first" is from the earlier exchange "The contest". Draft 16 puts the two together, as the brief asks.
16. **"The condition stays as a task".** His word is "is", not "stays": in draft 15 the condition was a field that names a rule, and no task (item 2).
17. **"the core cares only whether it has triggered".** "Only" is the MOR lead's, and the brief's. His words: "triggered or not is what the core cares about." Draft 16 follows the brief; item 1 is what follows from "only".
18. **"what it stops … [is] that cMIP's"** (the brief). His words in this exchange: "How it triggers is cMIP". That the condition itself says what it stops is draft 15's, from the exchange "What void changes", and is kept.
19. **"nothing built on either counts until it is resolved, and the last shared version stays in force".** His words: "what needs to happen is something that stops both tips until resolved." The rest is the MOR lead's reading, put to him; he did not object, and answered the next point. That the last shared version stays in force is from the earlier exchange "Forks".
20. **"signed as an update must be, that continues one tip".** His words: "It is resolved by making one of the two void with an update." The rest is the MOR lead's (item 9).
21. **"what others relied on in it stands, shown as disputed".** Not in this exchange; it is from the earlier exchange "Forks", and is kept as draft 15 has it (G12.5).
22. **"Two rival resolving updates are themselves a fork, resolved the same way."** Not in his words. It is what is kept of draft 15's proposal on two joins, in the MOR lead's line. Draft 16 writes it (G12.7) because the brief asks for it, and writes its cost (section 7, "A fork of Terms holds the agreement where it was").
23. **"a client duty".** His words say what a client should not do. "Client conformance", and that no verifier can check it, are the brief's (G12.8).
24. **"legibility only works if what is made legible can be contested".** His words: "Making greed legible does not work without contest, it makes it core." The line leaves out "greed". No grammar rule rests on the line; his words are quoted in section 5.1 and in `reasons-16.md`.

**8.3 Left as draft 15 has it**

Each item is where one of draft 16's changes touches something the three exchanges do not cover. Each is left as draft 15 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so.

**The contest (G11.8).**

1. **The signers' contest of their service.** From the earlier exchange: "If the signer believe the mistake is at the service they contest the service". Draft 15 placed that contest within the deal the signers have with the service, and draft 16 leaves it there. A deal is signed by parties who were known, so by the definition of section 2 it is not clearly an offer, and whether it is a vow is not said.
2. **What a buyer's contest names.** The version the payment read, and the payment, in `settlement`: as draft 15 has it.
3. **What a contest holds, and what it does.** What its `content` holds is not read. What a contest does beyond standing on the record, who answers it and how, and how money comes back, are not the grammar's: as draft 15 has it.
4. **"At first".** In the earlier exchange the signers "are contested at first". Draft 15's rule did not say "first" ("It reaches that agreement and its signers"); draft 16 says "and no one else", of the one contest. Whether "at first" means that the same contest later reaches further is not said.

**The condition (G10.7).**

5. **One condition for each version** (P21), and **which clock it reads** (P16): as draft 15 has them.
6. **A later version that offers again**, and that a verifier never answers for an act that never counted: as draft 15 has them, in the new words.
7. **What a verifier shows of what stops.** The least that lets the text be read: it MAY show what the cMIP says the triggering stops, as the cMIP's word. Whether it must is not said.

**Forks (G12).**

8. **A power to resolve, given in advance** (G12.4): as draft 15 has it, with "resolving update" where it said "join".
9. **What others relied on** (G12.5): as draft 15 has it. "Others" are still those who signed none of the acts of the branch set aside. What those who did sign them had built on it is not said, beyond that the tip never counted.
10. **More than two tips.** A fork is defined as two updates. Three updates of the same act are read as draft 15 read three branches: `resolves` names every tip.
11. **A resolving update carries no words of its own**: as draft 15's join that named `continues` (section 3).
12. **How a resolving update signed under a power sits in another signer's line** (G6.1, G12.4): open since draft 10, unchanged.
13. **Since when the continued tip counts.** G12.3 says that it then counts as the next act of the line. Whether what was built on it while the fork stood counts from when it was made, or from the resolving update, is not said; where it matters, it is an answer about order (G13.3, G13.4).

**The rest.**

14. **Everything draft 15 listed as open** in its sections 8.2 (items 5 to 11) and 8.3 is still open, but for what is noted in `history.md`, section I.5. It is summed up in section 8.4.
15. **Nothing was tested.** No test was run against draft 16.

# Part K. Draft 18

**Read for draft 18, and nothing else:** `docs/agreements-from-scratch/draft-17.md`, `reasons-17.md`, this file and `companion-3.md` (branch `claude/agreements-draft-17-hp0j90`, pull request 171); `docs/decisions.md` and `docs/lead/proposals/report-block-2.md` (branch `claude/lead-records-2`); `docs/agreements-from-scratch/seams-1.md` (branch `claude/agreements-seams-1`, pull request 172); and, from the repository `MOR-CBOX`, branch `claude/agreements-c-1` (its pull request 3), only `QUESTIONS.md`, `REPORT.md` and `STANDINS.md`. Of this file, only its headings and Part J were read. The old Agreements MIR, `docs/findings/`, `docs/agreements-inventory/` and `docs/agreements-marking/` were not opened; neither were the lower layers' texts, which are known here only as the seams quote them. Draft 18 is draft 17 with seven changes, and nothing else. Four exchanges of the ledger are brought in, each read as a whole: **"The update condition"**, **"The default update condition"**, **"A task is a socket"** and **"The same work, baked again"**. Nothing in `MOR-CBOX` was changed.

**The tests for every choice**, in the words of Nobody, allegedly: "this one supports and specifies the language for creating solutions", and "We need to think more in « tasks » and output. We need to think like MOR."

## K.1 The changes, and where each comes from

| | Where it comes from | What changed (Claude's reading) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A task is a socket.** The seams' X2: the layer that defines cMIPs allows one cMIP for each task in an agreement, and draft 17's Terms name several rules. He: "Is it truly the same task if it appears in a different field?" The MOR lead: no, each field asks its own question. He: "Are your fields what I have been calling tasks?" The MOR lead: yes. He: "Yes, task has always been the socket in my mind. One cMIP per socket, multiple modules where needed, possibly multiple sockets in one cMIP." | Every field of an act that names a cMIP is a task. K1 to K13 are rebuilt as seven sockets, K1 to K7, each with its one question, what it accepts, what it produces and where its output is used (section 5). A role is the spot task's input. One cMIP may fill several sockets. The other earlier "tasks" are listed as modules (section 5.3). One field is new, `control`, since the Control task had none (the C session's Q12). |
| 2 | **The same work, baked again.** The seams' X1: anyone may bake the same bytes, so a copier is the baker of their own bake and passes G14. The MOR lead leaned that MOR shows "same work, baked by others too" beside each bake, never picks, and the original baker can contest. He: "Why never pick?" The MOR lead: the bytes say who uploaded and when, not who made it; first is not author; first depends on the reader's anchoring; picking needs judgement, a cMIP's or an arbiter's. He: "Ok, but a client can chose to rank based on knots even if anchoring is absent" | G14.5, new: an answer a verifier gives beside a bake, with its reasoning; it never picks; a client may rank, by knots even where no anchoring is named; a cMIP or an arbiter may judge; a baker may contest. G14's lie and whom it protects; a new cost, "A copy is made legible, not forbidden". |
| 3 | **The default update condition.** Draft 17's section 8.2, item 2. He: "Everyone who signed the original is the default condition yes". Then, on whether a named condition may refuse what everyone signed: "Overruled by cMIP should not be something the core accepts. The core wants the result of the task as computed by the cMIP and plug it into whatever it needs to do", and "We need to think more in « tasks » and output. We need to think like MOR." | G4.8 is set out in clauses and says the default in his words; G4.11(e) says it; G4.11(i), new: the core takes the task's output, and has no notion of overruling and no value "locked". |
| 4 | **The seams' wiring**, X3 to X7: the MOR lead's technical wording, given in the brief. | **X3** (G11.4(b)): two halves are tied by the act both name, and, where the layer that holds value records one payment as two acts, by that layer's answer to "the same payment"; because in Money the two sides sign two different acts. **X4** (G11.4(c)): what Money counts toward a debt and what a reader of reports is shown are two questions; this text shows both halves and chooses neither, and Money's counting is Money's; because Money counts the greater amount and the block chooses neither. **X5** (G7.1; section 8.1): G7 reads a place as the layer that orders acts gives it, and what that layer must provide is listed; because no lower text gives a place. **X6** (section 2): "disputed" and "valid" each have one meaning here, with how each differs from the core's word; because one spelling carried two conditions. **X7** (section 3.2; section 8.1): the report is one act type, defined once in the shared language; because a report must be the same act in every layer. |
| 5 | **The C session's questions.** A C session built a verifier from draft 17 alone and recorded 37 places where it had to choose a reading, 24 of which change an answer. For its Q1 the brief gives his reading: a fork is two updates each signed by all who needed to sign; an assent to a tip counts for whether that tip was signed, and for nothing else. | Each of Q1 to Q24 is answered in the text (K.5). Of Q25 to Q37, ten are fixed and three listed (draft 18's section 8.3, item 14). |
| 6 | The brief's: for the box. | Section 9, new: "What a builder needs that is not in this text", fifteen things in order. |
| 7 | The brief's, as housekeeping. | The rules are in `draft-18.md`; the plain words and reasoning in `reasons-18.md`, which is `reasons-17.md` changed only where these changes touch it; this part is added here; `companion-3.md` is changed in three rows, one note and its head. `draft-17.md` and `reasons-17.md` stay in the repository. |

Draft 17 had fourteen grammar rules and thirteen tasks. Draft 18 has fourteen grammar rules and seven tasks. No grammar rule is added or removed. Clauses: G4.8 is set out as (a) to (f); G4.11(e) is rewritten and (i) is new; G9.2 is new; G14.5 is new; G1.2, G1.4, G2.2, G4.1, G4.7, G4.9, G4.10, G5.4, G5.5, G6.1, G7.1, G7.2, G8.2, G11.2, G11.4, G11.8, G12.1 to G12.3, G12.5, G12.7, G14.2 and G14.3 gain sentences. One field is added: `control`. None is removed or renamed. Definitions added: module, the original, knot, "carry the same work", "not an Agreements act". Definitions changed: task, cMIP, rule, settlement, valid, invalid, disputed, place, shares, fork, built on a tip. Answers: none added; **rejected** is named as no answer of this text. Parameters touched: P2, P8, P9, P17, P18, P19, P20, P21.

## K.2 Every number and word that changed

**Where each task number of drafts 8 to 17 went.**

| Until draft 17 | In draft 18 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| K1 Body | K1, the socket `body` | a field that names a cMIP |
| K4 Rule | no task: a rule is a kind of cMIP, or a module of one. The fields that held one are three tasks: K2 (`in_force`), K3 (each entry of `powers`), K4 (`condition`) | "Is it truly the same task if it appears in a different field?" |
| K8 Control | K6, the socket `control` (a new field) | the task had no field |
| K11 Kind | K7, a Statement's `kind` | a field that names a cMIP |
| K12 Spot | K5, the cMIP of each role; the role is its input | the brief: a role is the spot task's input |
| K2 Identity, K3 Assurance, K5 Witness, K7 Tally, K9 Delivery, K10 Judge | modules (section 5.3), with no number | "multiple modules where needed"; no field names them |
| K6 Place | not a task: the field `place` names a place, which is no cMIP | X5 |
| K13 Standing | not a task of an act: a reader chooses the cMIP | no act names it |

Since a number now means something else, records up to draft 17 keep their numbers and are read with this table.

**Words.**

| Draft 17 | Draft 18 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| "task": one question the grammar asks and does not answer | "task", or "socket": a field of an act where a cMIP plugs in and answers one question | his words |
| "a cMIP answers one task" | "a cMIP fills a socket"; one cMIP may fill several | his words |
| "rule: a cMIP answering task K4" | "rule: a cMIP, or a module of one, that computes" | a rule is no longer a task |
| the `settlement` field as "the socket where it plugs in" | "where it plugs in"; not a task | "socket" now means one thing |
| "provided by the Order layer" | "as the layer that orders acts gives it" | X5 |
| "no question is asked" where no condition is named | "the default update condition": valid when everyone who signed the original signs it | his words |
| "every party bound by them" (G4.8) | "everyone who signed the original" | his words |
| the lie "said by someone who copied its bytes" | the same, of one who names another's envelope; and "nobody else baked this" | G14.5 |

New words: **socket**, **modules**, **the original**, **knot** (his); **module** as a defined term, `control`, **rejected** (Claude's).

## K.3 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchanges

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check". Each item is something a line, or the brief, says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 18 does. Item numbers in brackets are draft 18's section 8.2.

**"A task is a socket".**

1. **"a place in an act where a cMIP plugs in and answers one question"**. The MOR lead's. His words: "task has always been the socket in my mind." That it answers one question is from the MOR lead's answer to his question about fields (item 16).
2. **"modules, several where needed"**. His words: "multiple modules where needed". What a module is, is not said. Draft 18 reads the earlier kinds of computation as modules (item 3).
3. **"one cMIP may fill several sockets"**. His words: "possibly multiple sockets in one cMIP". Draft 18 adds that it is asked each socket's question apart.
4. **"Draft 17's K1 to K13, kinds of computation, are rebuilt as the list of sockets, each with what it accepts and what it produces."** The MOR lead's. His words name no list (item 17).
5. **"Development's 'at most one cMIP per task' then holds as written."** The MOR lead's. Draft 18 did not read that text and says only "one cMIP per socket".

**"The same work, baked again".**

6. **"the core shows that bakes carry the same work and never picks between them"**. The MOR lead's lean; his "Ok" is read as taking it (item 18).
7. **"A client may rank them, by knots (the order acts give by citing) even where no anchoring is named"**. His words: "a client can chose to rank based on knots even if anchoring is absent". The gloss of "knots" is the MOR lead's; it agrees with his earlier "knots forming, order emerging".
8. **"a cMIP or an arbiter may judge; the original baker may contest"**. The MOR lead's; he did not speak to either (items 9 and 19). Draft 18 lets any baker contest, since no act shows which is the original.
9. **"The copy is made legible, not forbidden."** The MOR lead's. Draft 18 uses it as the name of a cost.

**"The default update condition".**

10. **"where Terms name no update condition, the default is that an update is valid when everyone who signed the original signs it"**. "Where Terms name no update condition" is the MOR lead's. His words: "Everyone who signed the original is the default condition yes".
11. **"There is no separate 'locked'"**, then **"no notion of overruling and no 'locked' value"**. The second replaces the first. Between them he said "Dangerous path… locked is best."; the last line follows the MOR lead's summary and his "We need to think more in « tasks » and output." Draft 18 follows the end of the exchange (item 8).
12. **"A lock needs nothing new: it is an update condition whose cMIP always answers not valid."** The MOR lead's. Draft 18 writes it in G4.11(i) and section 5.2.
13. **"Draft 17 already reads this way and needs no change for it."** The MOR lead's. Draft 17's G4.11(e) said "no question is asked"; draft 18 writes the default in his words, and leaves a power for replacement with no condition named as draft 17 has it (items 7 and 20).

**"The update condition".** Nothing new is taken from it; what draft 17 took stands (Part J, J.3).

**The brief.**

14. **"Every field of an act that names a cMIP is a task"**, **"a role is the spot task's input, not a task of its own"**, **"(`in_force`, each power, `condition`, each role's spot, and every other)"**. The brief's (item 21). "Every other" is read as: `body`, a Statement's `kind`, and the Control task, which needed a field.
15. **"Write it into G14 as an answer a verifier gives beside a bake, with its reasoning"**. The brief's form. The reasoning quoted, "the bytes say who uploaded and when, not who made it", is the MOR lead's.
16. **X3 to X7** are the MOR lead's technical wording. None is the words of Nobody, allegedly.
17. **"For Q1 the reading is his"**. The first half is his words in "A fork, from MOR's side"; the second half is the brief's statement of what follows (item 22).
18. **"the core takes the update condition's output and has no notion of overruling or locking"**. From the last arrived-at line, and his "The core wants the result of the task as computed by the cMIP".

Nothing in this table is a decision; each reading is for the MOR lead and for Nobody, allegedly, to check.

It is also the C session's | G12.2; G4.7; section 2, "built on a tip" |
| Q2 | A delegation ended by the delegator alone, and the new version's own `in_force` | Taken: the delegator's part alone settles both the signatures and the coming into force; so does the agent's alone, under a condition that leaves ending to the agent | G5.5; G12.1 |

Where nobody signed, as the words give it; listed as open | G4.8(a); section 8.2, items 5 and 6 |
| Q8 | A stranger's assent to a tip that is set aside "stands" | Taken: an assent or other Statement stands as an act, disputed, and binds no one; a payment is read as if the tip had stood alone, disputed; "the acts of a branch" are the acts of the line | G12.5 |

For Terms, which of the two a next version names is left open (section 8.3, item 8) |

## K.6 Draft 17's lists of open items, unchanged, and what draft 18 does with them

What follows the notes below is sections 8.2 and 8.3 of `draft-17.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 17" there mean draft 17; task numbers there are draft 17's (K.2).

What draft 18 does with them:

- **8.2, item 2** (everyone signed, and the condition answers not valid): answered by the exchange "The default update condition". It stays as written: the core takes the cMIP's output, and has no notion of overruling or locking (G4.11(i)). With no condition named, the default is everyone who signed the original (G4.11(e)).
- **8.2, item 9** (no task is added): overtaken. A task is a socket, and the update condition is the task K4, the socket `condition`. K4 (Rule) and K12 (Spot) there are draft 17's numbers.
- **8.3, item 3** (a power to resolve): unchanged; its rule is now the socket of that power (K3).
- **8.3, item 4** (the `in_force` rule): now the socket K2; otherwise not touched.
- **8.3, item 10** (nothing was tested): a C session has since built a verifier from draft 17 (K.5). No test was run against draft 18.
- Everything else there is still open.

**Section 8.2 of `draft-17.md`: For Nobody, allegedly**

Draft 17 is draft 16 with one change (`history.md`, Part J). One exchange is brought in, read as a whole: "The update condition". In plain words: an agreement can say, in one simple field, which updates it accepts. The cMIP that field names answers one question about a proposed update: can the agreement be updated like this, valid or not valid. Nothing triggers, and nothing changes by itself.

**What is left of each of the two things that are now one.**

- *Of the rule of a power for replacement (G5.2):* who may sign an update. It no longer answers about the update itself.
- *Of the condition (G10.7):* the field `condition`, which now names the update condition. Its answer **triggered** is gone, and so is everything G10.7 said about what a triggering stops. G10.7 keeps what was not about triggering: a filling stays a filling, and a later version may offer again.
- *The one block* is G4.11: given the version in force and a proposed update, valid or not valid.

**Choices this draft had to make.** Each is Claude's reading, for the MOR lead and for Nobody, allegedly, to take, change or refuse.

1. **Where the one place is.** The block is written as a new clause of G4, G4.11, beside the clause that says when new Terms replace old ones (G4.8). It could have stayed at G10.7, or gone into G5.2. G10 is about spots and G5 about who is bound; the update condition is about updating an agreement, which G4 reads. No clause number changes: G5.2 and G10.7 stay, each saying what is left of it.
2. **Everyone signed, and the condition answers not valid.** His words: "It answers wether it can be updated or not." Read plainly: where it answers not valid, the update replaces nothing, whoever signed it, every party included (G4.11(c)). *What it costs:* an agreement can be written that even all its parties together cannot update, or cannot update in some part. Whether everyone's signature should be enough against the condition is not said in the exchange.
3. **A filling's rights (G10.6).** In plain words: someone bought a licence under one version. Until draft 16, a later version could not touch it without their signature, except through the condition their own version named, when it triggered. With triggering gone, the same door is kept in the new words: a later version changes the rights of a filling, without its holder's signature, only where the update condition of the version the filling was earned under answers that later version valid (G10.6(b)). The exchange gives the first half, in the MOR lead's summary, to which he said "Yes, and the cMIPs replies.": rights stop "only because a valid update says so". That the condition asked is the one of the holder's own version is carried over from draft 16 ("under a condition which that filling's own version does not name"). *What it costs:* what a holder keeps is as safe as the update condition of the version they earned under, and no safer. Where that version names none, only their own signature changes it.
4. **Several versions between.** For a filling, G10.6(b) gives the condition "that version and the later one". Where other versions lie between, the condition of the holder's version is then asked about a version that does not replace it directly. The exchange speaks only of "the version in force and a proposed update" (the MOR lead's words). This is the least reading Claude found that keeps draft 16's guard.
5. **"The cMIP can run the verification via bytes, so it should be able to run."** Read as: the cMIP works from the bytes it is given, anyone who holds them can run it, on an update already signed or only proposed, and its answer waits for no one to publish anything (G4.11(b)). He said it of an offer closing on a date, against the MOR lead's reading that someone would have to publish a closing update. What this draft does not take from it: that rights stop with no update at all. The brief says rights stop applying only through an update the condition answers valid, and an update is an act that those who needed to sign it have signed (G12.1). So, as written, rights that stop need a signed update; whether a spot still takes new people needs none (section 8.3, item 1). If "it should be able to run" means that the stopping itself needs no act from anyone, the text says less than he meant.
6. **What the cMIP may read.** As any rule, the update condition's cMIP may read the acts held and the answers of the place and the clock, beside the two versions (G4.11(a)). His word is "bytes"; draft 16's condition had the same inputs, and they are left. Then a condition can say "no update before June". It also means that an answer can differ between readers who hold different acts (section 7).
7. **Valid and not valid, beside invalid.** The verifier's answers now include **valid** and **not valid**, his words, said of a proposed update. This text already had **invalid**, for an act that itself shows it cannot count. The two are kept apart (P19).
8. **A resolving update is not asked.** A resolving update carries no words of its own, and each tip it names was already answered valid, so the condition is not asked of it (G4.11(g)). The exchange does not speak of forks.
9. **No task is added, and none is left empty.** The MOR lead asked whether the condition should have a task of its own. His answer was "Condition is short for update condition", which does not say. The update condition stays a cMIP answering K4 (Rule); K4's row says what it is given and that it answers valid or not valid. K4 still has everything else it had, and K12 loses only its reading of triggering. There are still thirteen tasks, with their numbers.
10. **Words.** "Update condition", "valid" and "not valid" are his. "Proposed update" is the MOR lead's. "Triggered" was his word in the earlier exchange "The condition is a task"; it goes on the MOR lead's summary, to which he said yes. The field keeps its name, `condition`.

**Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchange.** The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's readings, marked "to check"; the last replaces the first where they differ. Draft 17 follows the exchange where they say more. The full list is in `history.md` (section J.3); the ones that change what a rule says:

11. **"given the version in force and a proposed update"**. The MOR lead's words, in his summary and in the brief. His own: "it is wether it is valid or not valid", and to the summary: "Yes, and the cMIPs replies." Draft 17 follows the summary he said yes to.
12. **"The power's rule (G5.2) and the condition (G10.7) are this one block."** The MOR lead's ("the same block as the power's rule"), in the summary he said yes to. In the earlier exchange "What the grammar is for", his words gave one cMIP both halves: "the cMiP builds a 2 out of 3 signature scheme" and "allowed to edit this task or not". That who may sign stays with powers, and only the answer about the update moves, is the brief's. Draft 17 follows the brief.
13. **"Nothing changes by itself: rights stop applying, or an offer stops taking new people, only through an update the condition answers valid."** The first line. Its second half was then put to him as a closing on a date, and he answered "No" (item 5). The last line withdraws the reading that a closing needs someone to publish an update. The brief says "an offer changes", and that whether a spot still takes new identities is not covered. Draft 17: what a version offers changes only through an update; whether a spot still takes new identities is left as draft 16 has it (section 8.3, item 1).
14. **"The answers 'triggered' and 'no longer applies' are dropped."** The MOR lead's, in the summary he said yes to. His own words in this exchange do not name either.
15. **"so nothing waits for anyone to publish"**. The MOR lead's. His words: "the cMIP can run the verification via bytes, so it should be able to run." (item 5).
16. **"The core keeps one simple field"**. His words: "the core still needs a simple field the cMIP can then specify in details." "One" is the MOR lead's, and the brief's. Draft 17 has one field, as draft 16 had (P21).
17. **"whether the agreement can be updated or not"**. His words: "It answers wether it can be updated or not." "The agreement" is the MOR lead's reading of "it".
18. **"with its parameters"**. The brief's. Draft 16's field already carried them.

**Section 8.3 of `draft-17.md`: Left as draft 16 has it**

Each item is where the change touches something the exchange does not cover. Each is left as draft 16 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so.

1. **Whether a spot still takes new identities (G10.5, K12).** Left as draft 16 has it, less the reference to triggering: the spot's own cMIP says, from the version in force, the acts held, the place and the clock. So an offer that says "open until June" stops taking new people in June with no update, as that cMIP reads the version. The update condition is not asked: the MOR lead's reading that it is asked of any act, a new claim included, is withdrawn. *Open:* the first arrived-at line said that an offer stops taking new people only through an update. His "the cMIP can run the verification via bytes" was said of exactly this case, and does not say which cMIP runs: the spot's, or the update condition's. And "nothing changes by itself" (G4.11(f)) is written of what a version offers and of earned rights, not of whether a spot is still open under the version's own words.
2. **A delegation's signed conditions (G5.5).** The Terms of a delegation may state conditions on the delegator's ability to end it (a notice, a term, a cause). Those are conditions on an update. Whether they are now to be written as the delegation's update condition, in `condition`, is not said. Left as draft 16 has it: read from the Terms, in the form their cMIP gives. Where a delegation's Terms also name an update condition, a version that ends it needs that answer too, as any update does (G4.8).
3. **A power to resolve (G12.4).** Its rule still says who may sign the resolving update "and which resolving update it accepts". The second half is an answer about an update, and would by the merge be the update condition's. The exchange does not speak of forks; G12 is not changed (section 8.2, item 8).
4. **The `in_force` rule (G4.1).** It says which assents put Terms in force. It is about who has signed, and is not touched.
5. **One condition for each version** (P21), and **which clock it reads** (P16): as draft 16 has them.
6. **A later version that offers again** (G10.7): as draft 16 has it.
7. **What a verifier shows of a change to a filling's rights.** The least that lets the text be read: the version the filling was earned under, each later version that changes its rights, and what that rests on (G10.6). What the change means is in the versions' words, which these grammar rules do not read.
8. **Changing the condition.** A new version may name another update condition, or none, if the condition before it answers that update valid (G4.11). Whether a condition may forbid its own change for good is that cMIP's; the grammar says nothing of it.
9. **Everything draft 16 listed as open** in its sections 8.2 and 8.3 is still open, but for what is noted in `history.md`, section J.5: the items about "triggered" are answered or gone. It is summed up in section 8.4.
10. **Nothing was tested.** No test was run against draft 17.

# Part L. Draft 19

**Read for draft 19, and nothing else:** `docs/agreements-from-scratch/draft-18.md`, `reasons-18.md`, this file and `companion-3.md` (branch `claude/agreements-draft-18-e2x9yo`, pull request 173); and `docs/decisions.md` (branch `claude/lead-records-2`). Of this file, only its head and Part K were read; of `companion-3.md`, its notes and the rows named below. The report block proposal, the seams, the C session's repository, the research texts and the lower layers' texts were not opened. Draft 19 is draft 18 with three changes, and nothing else. Two exchanges of the ledger are brought in, each read as a whole: **"Seven sockets, and data"** and **"Constitution, executive and judicial"**.

**The test for every choice**, in the words of Nobody, allegedly: "We need to think more in « tasks » and output. We need to think like MOR."

## L.1 The changes, and where each comes from

| | Where it comes from | What changed (Claude's reading) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Constitution, executive and judicial**, its first half. Draft 18's section 8.2, item 7: a power for replacement with no update condition named, which the default read strictly would refuse. The MOR lead put an example (three owners, "any two of us can sign an update", no update condition) and leaned that the power wins. He: "Not sure I follow"; "But the terms state that they did? I am confused". The MOR lead: the confusion is the draft's, from keeping "who may sign" apart from the update condition. He: "The update condition would be dictated by the 2 out of 3 signature cMIP, no? I am not sure why it could be simply done with what we had." | An update is governed only by the update condition. The cMIP in `condition` (K4) answers valid or not valid for a proposed update, who must sign it included; empty, the default is everyone who signed the original. The power for replacement (kind `replaces`) is removed: from `powers` (section 3.1), from K3, from G4.8, G4.11, G5.2 and G12.4. A party is bound by an update that the update condition of Terms binding it answers valid (G5.1(c), new). The condition is asked of a resolving update (G4.11(g)). |

Update equals constitution, and the judicial, power means the executional." Asked where the judicial sits (the MOR lead's guess: the contest, the judge module, the power to resolve a fork): "Yes, so changes to the judicial need an update, powers cannot touch it, no?" | G5.6, new, the one place that says it: the update condition is the constitution; powers are the executive; the judicial (the contest, a judge named in the Terms, a power to resolve a fork) is set in the Terms and changes only by an update; no power can change it. A power to resolve is of kind `resolves` only (G12.4). A judge is named in the Terms (section 5.3). A judge's decision still binds only through a power. |

The MOR lead: two acts, seven fields where a cMIP plugs in; the earlier kinds of computation are modules; one gap: the data cMIP for a path beyond one leg has no socket. He: "Wow… very lean. data is needed by so many processes , so yes I think a task is needed there." | One socket is added: K8, the field `data` in Terms, where a data cMIP is named. Its one question, what it accepts and what it produces are in section 5; G11.4(e) and G11.6(a) read it. |

Draft 18 had fourteen grammar rules and seven tasks. Draft 19 has fourteen grammar rules and eight tasks. No grammar rule is added or removed. One field is added: `data`. None is removed; the kind `replaces` is removed from the field `powers`. Definitions added: constitution, executive, judicial (one entry); data cMIP. Definitions changed: task (eight), report, never ended, power, condition, valid. Answers: none added or removed. Parameters touched: P15, P21. Costs: two added ("A judge is as hard to change as the Terms"; "Bound by an update one did not sign"); three reworded.

**Two slips of draft 18 mended.** The heading of section 5 of `draft-18.md` ("## 5. Tasks handed to cMIPs", as `draft-17.md` has it) and the heading of the last section of `reasons-18.md` were lost when those files were written. `draft-19.md` has the first back; `reasons-19.md` heads its last section "The tasks".

## L.3 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchanges

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check". Each item is something a line, or the brief, says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 19 does. Item numbers in brackets are draft 19's section 8.2.

**"Constitution, executive and judicial".**

1. **"an update is governed only by the update condition"**. "Only" is the MOR lead's. His words are a question: "The update condition would be dictated by the 2 out of 3 signature cMIP, no?" Draft 19 writes "only", as the brief asks, and leaves the new version's own `in_force` as draft 18 has it (item 17; section 8.3, item 1).
2. **"a cMIP in that socket (for instance two signatures out of three) answers valid or not valid"**. His example. That who must sign is part of the answer is the brief's "including who must sign it" (item 19).
3. **"empty, the default is everyone who signed the original"**. From the earlier exchange, "The default update condition"; not said again in this one.
4. **"The power for replacement goes; powers remain for every other act (acting for someone, a Statement of a kind)"**. The MOR lead's. He did not speak of which powers remain (item 18). Draft 19 keeps the power to resolve too, which the same line names under the judicial.
5. **"The update condition is the constitution; powers are the executive"**. His words: "Update equals constitution, and the judicial, power means the executional". The sentence can be read as putting the judicial with the update. The MOR lead then asked where the judicial sits, and he answered "Yes". Both readings give the same rule (item 20).
6. **"the judicial (the contest, the judge, the power to resolve a fork)"**. The MOR lead's guess; his word to it: "Yes" (item 21).
7. **"is set in the Terms"**. The MOR lead's and the brief's. His words say how the judicial changes, and end on "no?" (item 22). For the contest, which the grammar gives and no Terms create, draft 19 reads it narrowly: the Terms set whom a contest reaches (item 7).
8. **"no power can touch it"**. His: "powers cannot touch it". Draft 19 reads "touch" as "change", and writes that using what the Terms set (a judge's decision that binds through a power; a resolving update signed under a power to resolve; a contest made for someone) is not changing it (item 9). The exchange does not draw that line.
9. **"I think this difference recovers the Constitutional aspect of the old collectives."** His, and not in the arrived-at line. Draft 19 writes nothing about collectives for it (item 24).
10. **"I am not sure why it could be simply done with what we had."** Quoted as the ledger has it. Read as "could not"; a possible typo, for the MOR lead to run by him (item 15).

**"Seven sockets, and data".**

11. **"the seven sockets stand"**. The MOR lead's. His words: "Wow… very lean." (item 25).
12. **"A data task is added: a socket in Terms where a data cMIP is named, for a path beyond one leg that the people whose data it carries agreed to with their signature"**. The MOR lead's. His words: "data is needed by so many processes , so yes I think a task is needed there." "So many processes" may be wider than a path beyond one leg; draft 19 adds the socket for that path and nothing wider (item 26).

**The brief.**

13. **"including who must sign it"**. The brief's (item 19).
14. **"a power of kind `replaces`"**, and **"remove it from K3, G4.8, G5.2 and wherever else it is read"**. The brief's. "Wherever else" was: the `powers` row, the definition of a power, G4.11(d), (e) and (i), G12.3, G12.4, K3's row, section 5.2, P15 and two costs.
15. **"a judge named in the Terms"**. The brief's; the exchange says "the judge module" (item 23).
16. **"A judge's decision still binds only through a power, as draft 18 has it."** The brief's. Kept (G5.6(e)).
17. **"K8"**, **"its one question, what it accepts and what it produces"**, **"the least field name"**. The brief's form. The question, and the name `data`, are Claude's (items 11 and 16).

## L.5 Draft 18's lists of open items, unchanged, and what draft 19 does with them

What follows the notes below is sections 8.2 and 8.3 of `draft-18.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 18" there mean draft 18.

What draft 19 does with them:

- **8.2, item 7** (the default, and a power for replacement): answered by the exchange "Constitution, executive and judicial". There is no power for replacement; "any two of us" is an update condition's cMIP (G4.8, G4.11, G5.2).
- **8.2, item 1** (seven sockets): his words since: "Wow… very lean." An eighth is added (K8).
- **8.2, item 15** (words): unchanged; `data` is added to Claude's.
- **8.3, item 1** (a power for replacement with no update condition named): answered, as above.
- **8.3, item 3** (a socket for a data cMIP): answered: `data`, K8. A verifier no longer answers unchecked for whether Terms name one (G11.4(e)).
- **8.3, item 8** (a resolving update of Terms, and the next version): still open. Who signs a resolving update of Terms is now the update condition's answer (G4.11(g)).
- **8.3, item 16** (nothing was tested): still so. No test was run against draft 19.
- Everything else there is still open.

**Section 8.2 of `draft-18.md`: For Nobody, allegedly**

Draft 18 is draft 17 with seven changes (`history.md`, Part K). Four exchanges are brought in, each read as a whole: "The update condition", "The default update condition", "A task is a socket" and "The same work, baked again". In plain words:

- **A task is a socket.** Every field of an act where a cMIP plugs in is a task, and asks one question. There are seven (section 5). What earlier drafts called tasks and were really kinds of computation (a rule, a witness, a judge) are now modules a cMIP may use (section 5.3).
- **The same work, baked again.** A verifier shows, beside a bake, that others baked the same work too, and never picks between them (G14.5).
- **The default update condition.** Where Terms name no update condition, an update is valid when everyone who signed the original signs it (G4.8, G4.11(e)). The core takes the update condition's output and has no notion of overruling or locking (G4.11(i)).
- **The seams' wiring,** five things in the MOR lead's technical wording (below).
- **The C session's questions.** A stranger built a verifier from draft 17 alone and had to choose a reading in 37 places. Each of the 24 that change an answer now has its answer in the text (`history.md`, section K.5).

**The seams' wiring: what changed, and why, one line each.** These five are the MOR lead's technical wording, not the words of Nobody, allegedly.

- **X3, the tie** (G11.4(b)). *Changed:* two halves are tied by the act both name, and, where the layer that holds value records one payment as two acts, by that layer's answer to "the same payment". *Why:* in Money the payer and the receiver sign two different acts, so "the same act" alone would tie nothing.
- **X4, the greater amount** (G11.4(c)). *Changed:* what Money counts toward a debt and what a reader of reports is shown are said to be two questions; this text shows both halves, chooses neither, and says that Money's counting is Money's. *Why:* Money counts the greater of two amounts, and the report block chooses neither; both stand once they are seen to answer different questions.
- **X5, the place** (G7.1; section 8.1). *Changed:* G7 reads a place as the layer that orders acts gives it, and section 8.1 lists what that layer must provide. *Why:* no current lower text gives a place, so the need is written down for the later rewrite.
- **X6, "disputed" and "valid"** (section 2). *Changed:* each has one meaning in this text, and section 2 says how it differs from the core's word of the same spelling. *Why:* one spelling with two conditions tells a reader two different things.
- **X7, the report** (section 3.2; section 8.1). *Changed:* the report is one act type, defined once in the shared language, which this text uses. *Why:* a report must be the same act in every layer, so it cannot be defined once per layer, or as a kind of Statement here.

**Choices this draft had to make.** Each is Claude's reading, for the MOR lead and for Nobody, allegedly, to take, change or refuse.

1. **Seven sockets.** The fields of draft 17 that name a cMIP are six: `body`, `in_force`, each entry of `powers`, `condition`, each role, and a Statement's `kind`. The seventh is new (item 2). His words do not give a list; this one is read off the fields.
2. **A new field, `control`.** Draft 17 said Terms hold the Control task "where they name a cMIP that answers it", and no field named one; the C session had to look in `relies_on` (its Q12). If a task is a socket, that task needs a field. `control` is the least Claude found; its name is Claude's.
3. **What is left of the other six "tasks".** Rule, Identity, Assurance, Witness, Tally, Delivery and Judge are listed as modules (section 5.3), from his "multiple modules where needed". The exchange does not say what a module is. Standing has no socket: no act names it. The place and the clock are named by an identifier and are no cMIPs in this text, so `place` and `clock` are not tasks.
4. **No socket for a dividing rule.** Draft 17 spoke of a rule that Terms name for dividing, and had no field for it. Shares are now read from the body (K1) and divided by whichever cMIP needs them (section 2). Whether dividing should be a socket of its own is not said.
5. **"the original".** Read as the version an update would replace, not the root of the agreement. "Everyone who signed" it is read as: each party in whose name an assent to it counts, and each party bound by it through a power (G4.8(a), from the C session's Q7).
6. **Nobody signed the original.** Where Terms came into force under an `in_force` rule that asked for no one's assent, "everyone who signed the original" is no one, and any Terms act in force by its own `in_force` replaces them. The C session called that odd, and it is. It is written as the words give it (G4.8(a)) and listed here.
7. **The default, and a power for replacement.** His words: "Everyone who signed the original is the default condition yes". Where Terms give a power under which fewer than all may replace them and name no update condition, draft 17 lets an update so signed replace them. Read strictly, the default would refuse it, and such a power would do nothing without a named condition. Draft 18 leaves it as draft 17 has it: those the power's rule accepts sign for everyone the power binds (G4.11(e)). Whether he means the stricter reading is for him.
8. **"Locked".** The exchange ends on the MOR lead's summary (a lock is an update condition whose cMIP always answers not valid; no "locked" value) and on his "We need to think more in « tasks » and output. We need to think like MOR." Before it he had said "Dangerous path… locked is best." Draft 18 follows the end of the exchange: no "locked" value, and a verifier never says of itself that an agreement is locked (G4.11(i)).
9. **Which baker may contest.** The arrived-at line says "the original baker may contest". No act shows which baker is the original, so G14.5(e) lets any baker contest Terms for another bake of the same work, as that baker's word.
10. **"Carry the same work".** Left to the core: this text does not say how two bakes are known to hold the same bytes (section 8.1).
11. **A contest against any version.** The C session read a contest made against any version alike, whether or not its Terms name a party by role (its Q19). His words are "I contest an offer", and "or I contest a vow". Draft 18 takes the C session's reading because no answer turns on it, and shows beside the contest whether the version names a role (G11.8). If a contest may be made only against an offer or a vow, this is to change.
12. **Delegations are read from the body.** No field says that Terms are a delegation (the C session's Q3). Draft 18 takes its reading: the body's cMIP says so. *What it costs:* a verifier that cannot read the body of Terms that carry powers answers unchecked, where draft 17 seemed to answer not shown (G5.5).
13. **Numbers not fixed.** The C session's verifier follows eight levels of powers and receivers, 256 versions, and six rounds of reading. Draft 18 writes the answer (unchecked, beyond where a verifier stops) and not the numbers (G9.2; P2, P8).
14. **One place where the C session's reading is not taken as built.** Its Q18 compared an act's position with the position of the replacing Terms act, and called that a simplification. Draft 18 writes the position at which the replacing Terms came into force (G5.4).
15. **Words.** "Socket" and "modules" are his. "Knot" is his (G13.3, G14.5). "The original" is his. `control`, "module" in the singular as a defined term, and "rejected" are Claude's.

**Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchanges.** The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's readings, marked "to check". Draft 18 follows the exchange where a line says more. The full list is in `history.md` (section K.3); the ones that change what a rule says:

16. **"a place in an act where a cMIP plugs in and answers one question"**. The MOR lead's. His words: "task has always been the socket in my mind." That it answers one question is from the MOR lead's answer to his "Is it truly the same task if it appears in a different field?"
17. **"Draft 17's K1 to K13, kinds of computation, are rebuilt as the list of sockets"**. The MOR lead's and the brief's. His words name no list.
18. **"the core shows that bakes carry the same work and never picks between them"**. The MOR lead's lean. He asked "Why never pick?", and to the reasons given said: "Ok, but a client can chose to rank based on knots even if anchoring is absent". Draft 18 reads "Ok" as taking the lean for the core, and writes the client's ranking as his.
19. **"a cMIP or an arbiter may judge; the original baker may contest"**. The MOR lead's, in his lean and his reasons. He did not speak to either.
20. **"Draft 17 already reads this way and needs no change for it."** The MOR lead's. Draft 17's G4.11(e) said that with no condition named "no question is asked". Draft 18 writes the default in his words (items 5 to 7).
21. **"Every field of an act that names a cMIP is a task"**, and **"a role is the spot task's input, not a task of its own"**. The brief's.
22. **"an assent to a tip counts for whether that tip was signed, and for nothing else"**. The brief gives this reading as his. His words, in "A fork, from MOR's side": "two acts got signed by all parties that needed to sign their update." The second half is the brief's statement of what follows.

**Section 8.3 of `draft-18.md`: Left as draft 17 has it**

Each item is where a change touches something the exchanges and the MOR lead's wording do not cover. Each is left as draft 17 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so.

1. **A power for replacement with no update condition named** (G4.11(e)): as draft 17 (section 8.2, item 7).
2. **A socket for a dividing rule**: none (section 8.2, item 4). The division is computed by a module of whichever cMIP needs it.
3. **A socket for a data cMIP** (G11.6(a)): none. How Terms name one is still not said, so a verifier answers unchecked for whether signers agreed to a path beyond a leg (G11.4(e)).
4. **Standing, the place and the clock**: not sockets (section 5.3). Whether a clock is a module under another layer's cMIP is that layer's.
5. **The task of paying, in a payment** (G11.7): this text still says a payment holds a task; which field of a payment is its socket is Money's (section 8.1).
