## M.4 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchanges

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check". Each item is something a line, or the brief, says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 20 does.

**"An agent and the judicial".**

1. **"no"**, at the head of the line. The exchange: the MOR lead's lean was no; his reply begins "Right." Read as agreeing with the lean.
2. **"signing a party's assent to an update that changes the judge is within them unless the delegation says otherwise"**. The MOR lead's. His words say the agent operates "within the limits … that were handed to it"; they set no default. Draft 20 writes that the power's rule says how far the agent reaches (K3), and adds no default (G5.6(f); section 8.2, item 18).
3. **"the layer, the sockets, the cMIPs and the modules of its delegation"**. His words: "(layer. Sockets, cMIPs and modules)". Quoted as the ledger has it.
4. **"Typo confirmed: "could be simply done" is "couldn't be"."** The ledger's; draft 19's section 8.2, item 15 is answered by it.

**"The encoding first".**

5. **"no stand-in encoding"**. The MOR lead's. The exchange: the MOR lead proposed a provisional encoding; the relayed words ask whether it "should be properly defined first"; his "Yes, my choice." is to the order the MOR lead then proposed. Read as the line has it: draft 20 writes no encoding and lists what it must fix (section 10).
6. **"The grammar verifier in Rust is a later, larger step; the players may start once the encoding and its checker exist."** The MOR lead's; not in the exchange. Draft 20 writes nothing about it (section 8.2, item 19).

**"After box 2".**

7. **"as the MOR lead's wording"**. The exchange is his "Agreed" to the MOR lead's proposal. Draft 20 writes the answers as technical wording, the MOR lead's to change, and puts before him four readings that reach further than his words (section 8.2, items 10 to 13 and 20).
8. **"the report block's seams with the text are closed in a third proposal"**. The proposal said "the report block is brought in line". A third proposal is the line's form; draft 20 follows it (`report-block-3.md`).
9. **"no further text-only box round"**. The proposal's "rather than another text-only box"; his "Agreed". Draft 20 says so (section 9).

**The brief.**

10. **"Close each on the text's side where the text can".** Item 2 (the two sides) needed no change on the text's side: G11.1 already says it. Item 4 is closed by the least sentence: these grammar rules read a report as answering one call.
11. **"as precisely as a verifier needs: these sentences are what the vectors will be written from"**. Read as: each answer is written where a builder meets it, with the answer word and the case, not as a pointer to this file.

## M.5 Draft 19's lists of open items, unchanged, and what draft 20 does with them

What follows the notes below is sections 8.2 and 8.3 of `draft-19.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 19" there mean draft 19.

What draft 20 does with them:

- **8.2, item 10** (an assent made under a power, to an update that changes the judge): answered by "An agent and the judicial" (G5.6(f)).
- **8.2, item 14** (a buyer): still open; a report of the buyer's leg has no path beyond it (A26; G11.6(b)); put before him (draft 20's 8.2, item 12).
- **8.2, item 15** (a possible typo): answered by the ledger's note.
- **8.3, item 4** (a delegation's conditions beside its `condition`): answered by A33: where an update condition is named, it is the whole answer (G5.5).
- **8.3, item 14** (the report block not changed, and not read): read for draft 20; not changed; `report-block-3.md` proposes what it would need.
- **8.3, item 17** (nothing tested): the second C session built a verifier from draft 19; draft 20 answers its 33 readings. Nothing was tested against draft 20.
- From draft 18's list, carried in draft 19's 8.4: **which act a next version names after a resolving update** is answered (A11; G12.3); **what passes the baker's rights** is answered as far as the grammar goes (A29; G14.2(b)); **the position at which replacing Terms came into force** is answered (A10; G5.4, G13.3).
- Everything else there is still open.

**Section 8.2 of `draft-19.md`: For Nobody, allegedly**

Draft 19 is draft 18 with three changes (`history.md`, Part L). Two exchanges are brought in, each read as a whole: "Seven sockets, and data" and "Constitution, executive and judicial". In plain words:

- **An update is governed only by the update condition.** Whether a new version replaces the last one is one question with one answer: the update condition's, valid or not valid. Who must sign is part of that answer. A cMIP in that socket can say "any two of us three". With the socket empty, the default answers: everyone who signed the original. There is no longer a power for replacement (G4.8, G4.11, G5.2).
- **Constitution, executive and judicial.** The update condition is the constitution: how the Terms may change. Powers are the executive: who may act under the Terms, and in which kinds of act. The judicial (the contest, a judge named in the Terms, a power to resolve a fork) is set in the Terms and changes only by an update; no power can change it. It is said once, in G5.6.
- **A data task.** One socket is added, K8, the field `data` in Terms: where the people who sign those Terms name a data cMIP, and so agree to a path for their reports beyond one leg (G11.6(a)).

**What is left of each thing the power for replacement touched.**

- *Of the kind `replaces` in `powers` (section 3.1; K3):* nothing. The kinds of a power are a kind of Statement, or `resolves`. An entry of kind `replaces` gives no power and is left unread.
- *Of G4.8:* one test where there were two. Draft 18 asked for the signatures the original required and for the update condition's answer. Draft 19 asks for the answer alone. "Everyone who signed the original" stays, as the default.
- *Of G5.2:* who is bound by a replacing Terms act: whoever assented to it, and whoever is bound by the Terms it replaces where their update condition answers it valid (G5.1(c), new). Nothing is left of the power's rule.
- *Of G4.11:* (d) no longer says "never about who may sign"; (e) no longer reads a power for replacement beside the default; (g) now asks the condition of a resolving update.
- *Of G12.3 and G12.4:* a resolving update of Terms is signed as the update condition of the shared Terms asks, or under a power to resolve, which is of kind `resolves` and no longer a power for replacement.
- *Of G5.5:* the delegator's default is the default where the delegation names no update condition.
- *Powers remain for every other act:* an act made for a person or an agreement (G5.1(b)), an agreement's signature (G1.2), a Statement of a kind (K7), a resolving update (G12.4).

**What changes for the judicial, against draft 18.**

- *A power to resolve.* Draft 18 let a power for replacement rewrite `powers`, and so give, change or remove a power to resolve; and it read a power to resolve a fork of Terms as itself a power for replacement. Both go. A power to resolve is an entry of kind `resolves`, and it changes only by an update the update condition answers valid (G5.6, G12.4).
- *A judge.* Draft 18 did not say where a judge is named: a cMIP could have taken its judge from any act. Draft 19 says a judge is named in the Terms, by the cMIP in a socket or its parameters, and that a cMIP takes its judge from there and from no act made under a power (G5.6(c), (d)).
- *The contest.* Nothing changes: no power could change whom a contest reaches in draft 18 either. G5.6 says so.
- *A judge's decision* still binds only through a power, as draft 18 has it (G5.6(e)).

**Choices this draft had to make.** Each is Claude's reading, for the MOR lead and for Nobody, allegedly, to take, change or refuse.

1. **Bound by an update one did not sign.** If two of three can make an update, the third must be bound by it, or the two have made nothing. Draft 18 bound the third through the power. With the power gone, draft 19 adds G5.1(c): a party bound by Terms is bound by an update their update condition answers valid. The exchange does not say this; it follows from "The update condition would be dictated by the 2 out of 3 signature cMIP". Its cost is written in section 7.
2. **Terms that carry an entry of kind `replaces`.** Such an entry is left unread (G5.2). Terms written for draft 18 with "any two of us" as a power and no update condition are now updated only by everyone. Whether such an entry should instead make the Terms invalid is not said.
3. **Not valid, and not shown.** Under the default, an update that lacks a signature is **not shown** as a replacement: the missing assent may exist. Under a named cMIP that counts signatures, the same update is answered **not valid**, as the cMIP says, qualified as shown or per place (G4.11(c)). The two words for one situation are left so: the core takes the cMIP's output.
4. **The condition is asked of a resolving update** (G4.11(g)). Draft 18 did not ask it, and took the signatures from G4.8. Those signatures are now the condition's answer, so there is nowhere else to take them from. This keeps draft 18's result: where two of three could update, two of three can resolve.
5. **The delegator's default** (G5.5) is read as the default for a delegation whose Terms name no update condition. Where they name one, its cMIP answers. His words, from the earlier exchange: "The default is delegator can end it, but with provision that an agreement can add conditions to its ability to do so." Draft 18 was not clear whether the delegator's part alone was enough beside a named condition.
6. **Where it is said.** G5.6, under "Own signature", because what it adds is a limit on powers. His word is "executional"; the ledger's heading and the brief say "executive", and draft 19 writes that.
7. **The contest, "set in the Terms".** The contest is an act these grammar rules give; no Terms create it. What the Terms set is whom a contest reaches: the signers of the version it is made against. G5.6(c) says that and no more.
8. **Where a judge is named.** "In a socket of theirs, by the cMIP that socket names or by its parameters". No field is added for a judge. That a cMIP does not take its judge from an act made under a power is written as what a cMIP author must do; a verifier applies the cMIP and cannot see inside it.
9. **Using is not changing** (G5.6(e)). A power of kind `contest` (someone makes a contest for another) and a resolving update signed under a power are read as the executive at work, not as a power touching the judicial. The exchange does not draw this line.
10. **An assent made under a power, to an update.** An agent who holds a power of kind `assent` for a party may still assent, for that party, to an update, one that changes the judge included (G5.2). It is read as that party's signing, as draft 18 has it. Whether "powers cannot touch it" should reach that is for him.
11. **K8's one question.** "May this reader read this report, or what it states, beyond its own leg?", answered yes or no. The exchange gives no question. Everything else a data cMIP says stays its own (G11.6(a)).
12. **Two sides, two Terms.** A report carries the data of both sides of its leg. Where they are bound by different Terms, each Terms' data cMIP is asked, and the report is read beyond the leg only where every one says yes (G11.6(a)). That follows "the people whose data it carries"; the exchange does not say it.
13. **Empty `data`.** No path: one leg, as before.
14. **A buyer.** A buyer signs a payment and no Terms. The confirmation of the payment carries the buyer's data too. How a buyer agrees to a path for it is not said (section 8.3).
15. **A possible typo.** "I am not sure why it could be simply done with what we had." Draft 19 reads it as "could not", and quotes it nowhere as a rule. It is for the MOR lead to run by him.
16. **Words.** "Constitution", "judicial" and "executional" are his. "Executive" is the ledger's and the brief's. `data`, and "data cMIP" as a defined term, are Claude's; the field name is the MOR lead's to change.

**Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchanges.** The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's readings, marked "to check". Draft 19 follows the exchange where a line says more. The full list is in `history.md` (section L.3); the ones that change what a rule says:

17. **"an update is governed only by the update condition"**. "Only" is the MOR lead's. His words are a question: "The update condition would be dictated by the 2 out of 3 signature cMIP, no?"
18. **"The power for replacement goes; powers remain for every other act (acting for someone, a Statement of a kind)"**. The MOR lead's. He did not speak of powers that remain.
19. **"including who must sign it"**. The brief's. It is what his example of two out of three says.
20. **"The update condition is the constitution; powers are the executive"**. His words: "Update equals constitution, and the judicial, power means the executional". His sentence can also be read as putting the judicial with the update. The MOR lead then asked where the judicial sits, and he answered "Yes". Both readings give the same rule: the judicial changes only by an update.
21. **"the judicial (the contest, the judge, the power to resolve a fork)"**. The three are the MOR lead's guess. His word to it: "Yes".
22. **"is set in the Terms"**. The MOR lead's, and the brief's. His words: "so changes to the judicial need an update, powers cannot touch it, no?", which is a question, and says how it changes, not where it sits.
23. **"a judge named in the Terms"**. The brief's. The exchange says "the judge module".
24. **"I think this difference recovers the Constitutional aspect of the old collectives."** His, and not in the arrived-at line. Draft 19 writes nothing about collectives for it.
25. **"The seven sockets stand."** The MOR lead's. His words: "Wow… very lean."
26. **"a socket in Terms where a data cMIP is named, for a path beyond one leg that the people whose data it carries agreed to with their signature"**. The MOR lead's. His words: "data is needed by so many processes , so yes I think a task is needed there." "So many processes" may be wider than a path beyond one leg. Draft 19 adds the socket the line and the brief describe, for that path, and nothing wider.

**Section 8.3 of `draft-19.md`: Left as draft 18 has it**

Each item is where a change touches something the two exchanges do not cover. Each is left as draft 18 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so.

1. **The new version's own `in_force`** (G4.1, G4.5, G4.8). An update the condition answers valid replaces; the replacing Terms must still be in force by their own `in_force`, as before. So a version that two of three made valid, and that names all three as signers with no `in_force` rule, still waits for the third. Whether "governed only by the update condition" should reach that is not said.
2. **Terms that no one signed** (G4.8(a)): as draft 18.
3. **A filling's rights** (G10.6): still changed only by its holder's assent, or where the update condition of the version it was earned under answers valid for the later version. That the answer now includes who must sign changes nothing written there.
4. **A delegation's signed conditions** (a notice, a term, a cause; G5.5): still read from the body (K1), beside the update condition. Two places can now say how a delegation ends: its words, and its `condition`. Which one a verifier reads first is not said.
5. **An assent made for a party under a power** (G5.1(b), G5.2): counts as that party's signing of an update, as before (section 8.2, item 10).
6. **An agreement's signature** (G1.2): unchanged. An agreement named as a party signs an update as its own Terms require, under a power of its own or by all its signers.
7. **Rival resolving updates, and a fork as a veto** (G12.7; section 7): unchanged, now said of those the update condition accepts.
8. **A resolving update signed under a power, and the update condition** (G12.4): the condition is not asked of it, as before.
9. **A power to resolve a fork in one signer's own line** (G12.4): a power to make that act for the signer, and so a delegation the signer can end alone by default (G5.5). Ending it is an update, so G5.6(d) holds; that it needs the delegator alone is as draft 18 has it.
10. **A judge that a cMIP names inside its own text**, and not in parameters: fixed by the cMIP's fingerprint, so still named by the Terms (G2). No field for a judge is added.
11. **What a data cMIP is beyond its socket**: not said. Nor whether it belongs to Agreements alone (section 8.1), whose signature a path needs where a side of a leg signed no Terms (G11.2), or how a buyer agrees (section 8.2, item 14).
12. **"a rule in them under which others decide it"** (G11.6(b)): as draft 18. Whether that rule is the data cMIP's own, a power, or the update condition (naming a data cMIP later is an update) is not said.
13. **Data that is not a report** (Terms and their calls, what a payer learns of a work, standing): K8 is for reports beyond a leg only (section 8.4, from draft 14's list).
14. **The report block** (`report-block-2.md`) is not changed by this draft, and was not read for it.
15. **The modules** (section 5.3): unchanged, but for one sentence on the judge.
16. **Everything draft 18 listed as open** in its sections 8.2 and 8.3 is still open, but for what is noted in `history.md`, section L.5: its item 7 of 8.2 and items 1 and 3 of 8.3 are answered. It is summed up in section 8.4.
17. **Nothing was tested.** No test was run against draft 19. The C session's verifier was built from draft 17.

# Part N. Draft 21

**Read for draft 21, and nothing else:** `docs/agreements-from-scratch/draft-20.md`, `reasons-20.md`, this file and `companion-3.md`, and `docs/lead/proposals/report-block-3.md` (branch `claude/agreements-draft-20`, pull request 175); and `docs/decisions.md` (branch `claude/lead-records-2`). Of this file, its head and Part M were read. Not opened: the old Agreements MIR, `docs/findings/`, `docs/agreements-inventory/`, `docs/agreements-marking/`, the research texts and the lower layers' texts. Draft 21 is draft 20 with five changes, and nothing else. Four exchanges of the ledger are brought in, each read as a whole, with all their lines: **"A late rival"**, **"Setting aside what an agent made"**, **"The buyer signs the offer"** and **"A thief's Terms"**. In each, the last arrived-at line replaces the earlier ones, and the MOR lead's withdrawn leans are not used. They answer draft 20's section 8.2, items 10 to 13.

**The principles for every choice**, in the words of Nobody, allegedly: "this one supports and specifies the language for creating solutions", and "We need to think more in « tasks » and output. We need to think like MOR." And: "My words matter less than our words within that conversation do."

## N.2 Where the ledger's arrived-at lines, and the brief, say more than the exchanges

The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's, marked "to check". Each item is something a line, or the brief, says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 21 does.

**"A late rival".**

1. **"Where they show it, the update replaces nothing and is no tip of a fork. Where they do not, it is a fork, as draft 20 has it."** The MOR lead's lean, which his words answer by naming what can show the order. Read as taking the lean; written in G4.7.
2. **"only knots (what acts cite) or anchoring can show"**. Which knots, and which anchoring, the exchange does not say. Draft 21 reads every naming G13.1 reads, and the place and clock the agreement names (G4.7(a), (b); draft 21's 8.2, items 1 and 2).
3. **"For the previous one, the likelihood that they interacted with enough acts to set an order is pretty high"** and **"We’ll have data after the simulation, 1000 extra identities will be deployed, pros, prosumers and consumers. We’ll get a better idea of what knots can do…"** His; not rules. Draft 21 writes nothing for them; `reasons-21.md` quotes them under G4.

**"Setting aside what an agent made".**

4. **The first two arrived-at lines** ("the grantor may set aside an act made under the grant …"; the scope "pending his answer") are replaced by the last, and not used. So is the MOR lead's "until complete" lean, which the last line withdraws, and his first lean (an agent may withdraw, within its limits).
5. **"Whether the grantor may also withdraw, by counter signature, an assent the agent made before an agreement is complete (A15) follows the same reading: the grantor's act is an update of what was made in its name."** The MOR lead's. His words do not speak of the counter signature before completion. Draft 21 does not call a counter signature an update; it says what follows from his words with the rules the text has (G4.10, G5.7(c); draft 21's 8.2, items 5 and 10).
6. **"grantee"**, his word, corrected with him to "grantor" ("Yes. I did mean the grantor, sorry."); the ledger's typo list records it.

**"The buyer signs the offer".**

7. **"So the buyer is a signer like any other"**. The MOR lead's; his "Exactly" follows it. Taken.
8. **"a path beyond the leg for the buyer is whatever the offer's own Terms (their data socket) name"**. Followed. Draft 21 also asks the Terms of a receiving side reached through another agreement, as G11.6(a) already reads any report carrying the data of people under different Terms (draft 21's 8.2, items 6 and 11).
9. **The MOR lead's lean** (a buyer who wants to see further signs up to a data path): withdrawn by the exchange, not used.

**"A thief's Terms".**

10. **The first arrived-at line** ("Terms that rule a baked envelope count only if whoever holds the baker's rights is a party … invalid: it never counts, for anything, its receiver included") is replaced by the last. What it said of "for anything, receiver included" is the meaning of "invalid" in this text (section 2), and the brief asks it; draft 21 writes it.
11. **"Draft 20's narrower reading misread that rule; the grammar is to say it plainly."** Followed (G14.3). His word is "party"; G14.2 asks "bound by them", an assent in the party's name (draft 18's reading). Kept; its cost is listed (draft 21's 8.3, item 8).
12. **"should be made valid"**, his, corrected with him to "invalid" ("Invalid yes, sorry."); the ledger's typo list records it.
13. **The MOR lead's lean** (keep draft 20's reading): not used.

**The brief.**

14. **"Say which knots count, as precisely as a verifier needs."** Read as: name the fields, the acts, and the test, so that two builders give the same answer on the same acts (G4.7(a)); vectors for it are section 10, item 17.
15. **"Take out any reading in draft 20 that sets an agent's act aside."** None was found after Terms are in force. Before, the counter signature is kept and said not to set anything aside; an agent's act that is a tip of a fork is the fork rule, left as draft 20 has it (draft 21's 8.3, item 5).
16. **"Rework G11.6 and G11.7, and any place that read the buyer otherwise"**: also section 2 ("Buyer", "Payment"), G11.1, G11.4(e), and the cost "One leg, and no further".
17. **"Where these touch `report-block-3.md`"**: they do not (draft 21's section 8.1).

## N.4 Draft 20's lists of open items, unchanged, and what draft 21 does with them

What follows the notes below is sections 8.2 and 8.3 of `draft-20.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 20" there mean draft 20.

What draft 21 does with them:

- **8.2, item 10** (A6, a late rival): answered by "A late rival" (G4.7).
- **8.2, item 11** (A15, withdrawing an assent made by an agent): answered by "Setting aside what an agent made" (G5.7, G4.10).
- **8.2, item 12** (A26, a buyer's path beyond the leg): answered by "The buyer signs the offer" (G11.6(b), G11.7).
- **8.2, item 13** (A2, the Terms of a thief): answered by "A thief's Terms" (G14.3).
- **8.2, items 14 to 17** (a pending payment, money still owed then paid, pools, the modules): still open, kept in draft 21's section 8.2 under the same numbers.
- **8.3, item 13** (nothing tested against draft 20): nothing was tested against draft 21 either.
- Everything else there is still open.

**Section 8.2 of `draft-20.md`: For Nobody, allegedly**

Draft 20 is draft 19 with five changes (`history.md`, Part M). Three exchanges of the ledger are brought in, each read as a whole: "An agent and the judicial", "The encoding first" and "After box 2". In plain words:

- **The second C session's questions are answered in the text.** A stranger built a verifier from draft 19 alone and had to choose a reading in 46 places; 33 of them change an answer a verifier gives (A1 to A33). Each now has its answer written where a builder meets it, as precisely as a verifier needs, so that the question no longer arises. The table is in `history.md`, section M.2. Of Part B (B1 to B13), the form is fixed where it was cheap, and the rest is listed (section M.3).
- **The seams with the report block are closed on this text's side**, and what the block would need is written as a proposal, `report-block-3.md`, for the MOR lead (section 8.1).
- **What could not be decided at all** (a pending payment, money still owed and then paid, pools, the modules) is listed below, items 14 to 17, with what the encoding or a cMIP would need to settle each.
- **An agent acts within the limits handed to it.** An agent who holds a power to assent for a party may assent, for that party, to an update that changes the judge, where the power's rule says yes; the update still needs the update condition's answer. In his words: "Your risk, your dial" (G5.6(f)).
- **The encoding comes next.** Section 10 lists every place where this text leaves a choice to the bytes, so that the encoding session can start from it. In his words: "I think it’s best to do this properly first."

**What the three exchanges answer, of draft 19's list.**

- *Its 8.2, item 10* (an assent made under a power, to an update that changes the judge): answered by "An agent and the judicial". It is that party's signing where the power's rule says yes, and the update still passes the update condition (G5.6(f)).
- *Its 8.2, item 15* (a possible typo): answered. The ledger records his correction: "could be simply done" is "couldn't be simply done".
- *Its 8.3, item 17, and section 9, items 1 and 15* (nothing tested; the act as bytes; vectors): the order is now the ledger's: this draft, then the encoding of the new acts, its checker and vectors, then the full box; no further text-only box ("After box 2").

**Where draft 20 does not take the second C session's reading.** The brief asks that its reading be taken unless it contradicts an exchange or the text's own principles. Eight were not taken as built, and one was made exact. Each is Claude's reading, for the MOR lead and for Nobody, allegedly, to take, change or refuse.

1. **A5, "built on a tip".** The C session read it one naming deep. Draft 20 reads it as far as the naming goes (section 2; G12.2). Why: "nothing built on either tip counts", and his "something that stops both tips until resolved" ("A fork, from MOR's side"); an act two namings away would otherwise count while the act it rests on does not.
2. **A9, the counter signature under an `in_force` cMIP.** Taken, and made exact in one case: where an assent names the counter signature but the Terms could have been in force without it, the order is **not shown**, where the C session answered "before" (G4.10, G13.3). Why: the text's own "where neither names the other, … not shown".
3. **A10, when replacing Terms came into force.** The C session took the later of the replacing act's position and each named signer's first assent. Draft 20 takes the first position at which the replacing Terms are valid and in force, counting only what the place shows up to it (G5.4, G13.3). Why: the C session's reading leaves out the update condition, which alone decides whether the version replaces (G4.8); under the defaults the two agree.
4. **A14, an agreement that signs by all its signers.** The C session answered unchecked for any act but an assent. Draft 20 says which acts are one signing: the same act but for the signer and its own line (G1.2). Why: unchecked is said where a rule cannot be applied for lack of something (G9); here nothing was lacking but the sentence.
5. **A15, an agreement's assent and a counter signature.** The C session let a counter signature cancel only its own signer's assent made in their own name. Draft 20 also lets one made for a party under a power of kind `counter_signature`, or as an agreement's signature, cancel the assent counted in that party's name (G4.10). Why: section 3.1 already lists `counter_signature` as a kind of power, which the C session's reading would leave with nothing to do.
6. **A20, `previous` that names a record which is no act of an agreement.** The C session read it as an act not held. Draft 20 answers **invalid** (G6.1). Why: the verifier holds what is named, and unchecked is for what it lacks.
7. **A28, a baker who fills a role.** The C session answered not shown always. Draft 20 says G14.2(a) is met where the acts held show the baker filling the role, with an assent in the baker's name (G14.3). Why: G10.3, "Each signer who fills a spot is a party in it from then on".
8. **A30, a payment that names a version further along a branch set aside.** The C session answered not shown, as a limit of its build. Draft 20 says what "as if the tip had stood alone" means, so that it can be read (G12.5). Why: G12.5 already says such a payment is read so.
9. **A21 to A24, A27,** and the rest: taken, and written where each arises (`history.md`, section M.2). In A24 one thing is added: a reader is also a side, for silence, where it is the receiver for the act of an agreement the Terms name as a party (G11.4(d)), as G11.2 reads a receiving side.

**For him, undecided.** No answer to A1 to A33 was found to change something Nobody, allegedly, decided. Four are put before him all the same, because the reading reaches further than his words do. Each is written in the text as the least reading; none is decided.

10. **A6, a late rival.** Any two updates of the same Terms are a fork, however late the second: two of three, where the update condition lets two of three update, can take an agreement back to a version long since replaced, and every version since stops until the fork is resolved (G4.7; section 7). The way out can be said in the language: an update condition that answers not valid for an update whose original is no longer the last version (section 5.2). Whether he wants the grammar itself to refuse a late rival is for him.
11. **A15, withdrawing an assent made by an agent, or by an agreement.** His words: "Yes, a counter signature is a good idea. Nothing counts until all parties have signed". Draft 20 reads the counter signature as available to whoever may assent in a party's name (point 5). Whether he means that is for him.
12. **A26, a buyer's path beyond the leg.** His words: "Leg by leg by default. Buyer needs to receive confirmation payment has been received." A buyer signs a payment and no Terms, so draft 20 answers no path beyond the leg for a report of the buyer's leg, whatever the receiving side's Terms name (G11.6(b)). "By default" may mean that a buyer could agree to more; how is not said.
13. **A2, the Terms of a thief.** His words, of a lone sale: "That’s when we look at the envelope of the work. Who baked it? So, the thief should be at least forced to edit it and bake it again." Draft 20 reads "invalid" in G14.3 as an answer about the work: Terms for a baked work that name neither the baker nor a succession are still read by every other grammar rule (a receiver among them), and every answer about the work that rests on them says that they cannot count for it (section 2; G14.3). Whether he wants such Terms to count for nothing at all is for him.

**What could not be decided from the text at all.** The second C session left four things unbuilt because nothing in the text or below it says them. Each stays open; what would settle it:

14. **A payment that is pending** (section 9, item 7). No answer word exists for it here. *To settle it:* the Money layer's encoding says whether a payment or a settlement has a pending state, as which act, and what it names; then a cMIP of K6, or the encoding of the answer words (section 10), says what a verifier shows while it lasts. The least reading until then is unchecked (G9; section 8.3).
15. **Money shown as still owed, and later paid** (P20). *To settle it:* the encoding of a leg report's lines (K6's row) gives a "still owed" line something a later line can name (the report it was stated in, and the amount and to whom); then a cMIP of K6 says whether a later report's "passed on" line, naming it, closes it. Until then a "still owed" line is shown as stated and tied to nothing.
16. **Pools** (P6). *To settle it:* a cMIP of K6 for the pool, with a rule as its module, says when it is divided and how a claim is counted; and the encoding of a leg report says how one line names several settlements, or one settlement covers many counts, and whether one report answers several calls (the block's open point 3). Until then one report line names one settlement.
17. **The modules** (a judge, a tally, identity, assurance, delivery, standing; section 5.3). None is a socket. *To settle each:* a cMIP in a socket that names it as a module, and the encoding of what that cMIP is handed and gives back (section 10); for the judge, also where in the Terms it is named (G5.6(c)). Nothing in the grammar needs to change for any of them.

**Where the ledger's arrived-at lines say more than the exchanges.** The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's readings, marked "to check". Draft 20 follows the exchange where a line says more. The full list is in `history.md`, section M.4; the ones that touch what a rule says:

18. **"An agent and the judicial": "unless the delegation says otherwise".** The MOR lead's. His words: "The agent is operating within the limits (layer. Sockets, cMIPs and modules) that were handed to it." Draft 20 writes that the power's rule says how far the agent reaches, and that a verifier reads its output (G5.6(f)); it writes no default of its own beyond that.
19. **"The encoding first": "The grammar verifier in Rust is a later, larger step; the players may start once the encoding and its checker exist."** The MOR lead's; the exchange does not say it. Draft 20 writes nothing about it.
20. **"After box 2": "as the MOR lead's wording".** The exchange is his "Agreed" to the MOR lead's proposal. Draft 20 writes the answers to the 33 as technical wording, the MOR lead's to change, and lists where a reading reaches further than his words (items 10 to 13).

**Words.** "Built on a tip … however many namings lie between", "one signing", "who is a side, for silence", "as if the tip had stood alone" (as now defined) and "rejected" for a field of the wrong kind are Claude's. "Your risk, your dial" is his.

**Section 8.3 of `draft-20.md`: Left as draft 19 has it**

Each item is where a change touches something the three exchanges and the MOR lead's wording do not cover. Each is left as draft 19 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so.

1. **How a role is named** by `authors` and `receiver`. Section 3.1 lets both name a role and gives no way to point at one; the C session gave each role a label. Left to the encoding (section 10).
2. **The new version's own `in_force`** (G4.1, G4.8): as draft 19 (its 8.3, item 1).
3. **Terms that no one signed** (G4.8(a)): as draft 19.
4. **An assent that is a tip in its signer's own line** (P9): as draft 19. Whether its signer is bound meanwhile is not said.
5. **The numbers at which a verifier stops** (G9.2; P2, P8): not fixed; a fourth thing it may stop at is now named (counter signatures that lean on one another), and its number is not fixed either.
6. **A report in no line** (section 3.2, from B3): these grammar rules read none; whether the block makes a report stand in its signer's line is the block's.
7. **Who the judge is** (G5.6(d), B10): still a duty of the cMIP author; a verifier applies the cMIP and cannot see inside it.
8. **G3.2 and G12.8** (B11): duties of cMIPs and of clients, which a verifier does not check.
9. **A delegation's conditions beside a named update condition** (G5.5): where both are named, the update condition is the whole answer, and the words' conditions are read only by its cMIP (from A33). Whether people who wrote a notice in the words expected it to be read by the grammar is not said.
10. **A filling within a branch set aside** (G12.5, from A31): no later version is named as changing its rights. Whether the continued line's versions should reach it is not said.
11. **A payment that is pending** (draft 19's 8.3, item 11): the least reading is still unchecked (item 14 above).
12. **Everything draft 19 listed as open** in its sections 8.2 and 8.3 is still open, but for what is noted in `history.md`, section M.5. It is summed up in section 8.4.
13. **Nothing was tested against draft 20.** The second C session's verifier was built from draft 19. The next step is the encoding and vectors, not a test of this text alone.

# Part O. Draft 22

**Read for draft 22, and nothing else:** `docs/agreements-from-scratch/draft-21.md`, `reasons-21.md`, this file and `companion-3.md` (branch `claude/agreements-draft-21-hngk34`, pull request 176); and `docs/decisions.md` (branch `claude/lead-records-2`). Of this file, its head and Part N were read. Not opened: the old Agreements MIR, `docs/findings/`, `docs/agreements-inventory/`, `docs/agreements-marking/`, the research texts, the report block's proposals and the lower layers' texts. Draft 22 is draft 21 with one change, and housekeeping, and nothing else. One exchange of the ledger is brought in, read as a whole, with all its lines: **"The baker's rights after a sale"**. It answers draft 21's section 8.3, item 8.

**The principles for every choice**, in the words of Nobody, allegedly: "this one supports and specifies the language for creating solutions", and "We need to think more in « tasks » and output. We need to think like MOR." And: "My words matter less than our words within that conversation do."

## O.1 The change, and where it comes from

| | Where it comes from | What changed (Claude's reading) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The baker's rights after a sale** (draft 21's 8.3, item 8: "bound by them" in G14.2 read as an assent in that party's own name). The MOR lead leaned that the baker must be a party and bound, in any of the usual ways. He: "How are the bakers right handled by the core at the moment? It’s a matter of having them travel if needed". Then: "It creates a problem according to the session … if the true baker has to be involved after selling them. So, not wether the core can solve it, but wether a rightscMIP can handle it in a way where the core accepts the update without the baker if the rights were sold". The MOR lead: yes, once "bound by them" means bound in any way the grammar allows. He: "Agreed" | G14.2: "bound by them" means bound in any way G5.1 allows, by an update condition the party signed into the original included (G5.1(c)), not only by an assent in that party's own name; a rights cMIP in the body's socket (K1) passes the baker's rights, and once they are passed the baker is needed in no later agreement of the succession, and, in an agreement that names her, need only be bound. The update condition's answer is read, for this question, before and apart from G14.3's answer about the same Terms; the Terms replaced are asked G14.3 as any. G14.3(b): a baker who fills a role meets (a) where she is bound as G14.2 reads it. G14's protects line; a row in section 5.2; P18. |
| 2 | **Kept as draft 21 has it** (the MOR lead's settlement, from draft 21's section 8.3, named in the arrived-at line) | Terms where the baker's place is not yet shown count for nothing until it is shown (G14.3(b)); draft 22's section 8.3, item 1. |
| 3 | The brief's, as housekeeping | The rules are in `draft-22.md`; the plain words and reasoning in `reasons-22.md`, which is `reasons-21.md` changed only in its head and one passage under G14, marked "(draft 22)"; this part is added here; `companion-3.md` is changed in its heading, one note and row #11; section 10 gains to item 12, and an item 18. `report-block-3.md` is not touched, so no fourth proposal is written. `draft-21.md` and `reasons-21.md` stay in the repository. |

**In one line, what changed:** "bound by them", for the baker and for whoever holds her rights, now reaches a party bound by an update its condition answers valid, so the core accepts an update without the baker once her rights are passed and the holder is bound by it.

Draft 21 had fourteen grammar rules and eight tasks; so has draft 22. No clause, task, field, answer word or defined term is added or removed. Clauses that gain sentences or are rewritten: G14.2 (its paragraph on "bound by them"), G14.3(b) (one phrase). One row in section 5.2. P18. No cost in section 7 changes.

The arrived-at line is the MOR lead's, marked "to check". Each item is something the line, or the brief, says that the exchange, read as a whole, does not itself say, with what draft 22 does.

Taken. That an update needs no act of the baker is the update condition's answer (K4); draft 22 says one cMIP may fill both, as section 2 already allows (draft 22's 8.2, item 5).
3. **"once sold, the true baker is no longer needed".** His words: "the core accepts the update without the baker if the rights were sold". Draft 22 writes what follows in G14.2 and nothing more: a sale takes nothing else from her (draft 22's 8.2, item 8; 8.3, item 4).

## O.4 Draft 21's lists of open items, unchanged, and what draft 22 does with them

What follows the notes below is sections 8.2 and 8.3 of `draft-21.md`, moved and not edited. "This text" and "draft 21" there mean draft 21.

What draft 22 does with them:

- **8.3, item 8** ("bound by them", for the baker): answered by "The baker's rights after a sale" (G14.2).
- **8.2, item 12**, where it says G14.2 asks "an assent counting in their name": overtaken by the same answer; the rest of the item stands.

**Section 8.2 of `draft-21.md`: For Nobody, allegedly**

Draft 21 is draft 20 with five changes (`history.md`, Part N). Four exchanges of the ledger are brought in, each read as a whole: "A late rival", "Setting aside what an agent made", "The buyer signs the offer" and "A thief's Terms". In each, the last arrived-at line replaces the earlier ones, and the MOR lead's withdrawn leans are not used. In plain words:

- **A late rival.** An update to a version that had already been replaced replaces nothing, where the knots (what acts cite) or an anchoring show that it came after the replacement. Where they do not show it, it is a fork, as draft 20 has it. In his words: "Only the knots or anchoring can" (G4.7).
- **Nothing an agent made is set aside.** The one an agent acted for changes what the agent made only by an update, under the update rules the text already has; whoever agreed to what the agent signed keeps what that version gave them. In his words: "It’s never set aside, it’s always update." (G5.7, new).
- **The buyer signs the offer.** A buyer signs the offer's Terms, and paying is what those Terms require; the buyer's identity was only unknown when the offer was signed by its maker. So a path beyond the buyer's leg is whatever the offer's own data socket names (G11.6(b), G11.7).
- **A thief's Terms.** Terms that point to an envelope without the baker, or the holder of the baker's rights, as a party are invalid: they never count, for anything, their receiver included. The attempt cannot be stopped, and stays readable. In his words: "An agreement that points to an envelope already needs the baker (or the holder of the bakers rights) to be valid" (G14.2, G14.3).

**What the four exchanges answer, of draft 20's list.** Its 8.2, item 10 (A6, a late rival): answered by "A late rival" (G4.7). Item 11 (A15, withdrawing an assent made by an agent): answered by "Setting aside what an agent made" (G5.7; G4.10). Item 12 (A26, a buyer's path beyond the leg): answered by "The buyer signs the offer" (G11.6(b)). Item 13 (A2, the Terms of a thief): answered by "A thief's Terms" (G14.3). Draft 20's 8.2 and 8.3 are kept, unchanged, in `history.md`, section N.4; its items 14 to 17 are still open, and are kept below under their numbers, since other sections point to them.

**What follows, and is Claude's.** Each is written in the text as what the exchange asks of a verifier, as precisely as a verifier needs. None is decided; each is for the MOR lead and for Nobody, allegedly, to take, change or refuse.

1. **Which knots count** (G4.7(a)). Every naming G13.1 reads, in every act held, of any agreement, and a report's naming of its call and what it relied on. A late update is shown late where the update itself, or an assent without which it is not an update, names, directly or through acts that name it, enough acts for the replacing version to be valid and in force. That is G13.3's reading of "came after Terms came into force", used here; the exchange names knots, and does not say which.
2. **Which anchoring counts** (G4.7(b)). The place the replaced version names, as for any replacement (G4.8(d)); the clock the agreement names, where it orders the two events; and, only where neither is named, the reader's own anchoring. A time written in an act does not count (G8.2).
3. **The answer about a late update** (G4.7(c)). No new answer word: "replaces nothing, and is no tip", with G4.7 named, and with what shows it late, qualified as shown or per place, per clock or per anchoring. Where an act it names is lacking and might show it late, unchecked.
4. **A late resolving update** (G12.7). A resolving update of Terms is an update of the shared Terms, so one that the knots or an anchoring show came after another resolved the fork is late, and resolves nothing. That applies the exchange to a case it does not name.
5. **The counter signature, before Terms are in force** (G4.10, G5.7(c)). The exchange is about acts after an agreement is complete; the last arrived-at line reaches the counter signature before it. What follows from "never set aside, always update", read with the rules the text already has: before Terms are in force nothing counts for anyone (G4.10), so a counter signature takes nothing from anyone who agreed; it cancels the assent counted in a party's name whoever made it, the agent included, and sets nothing aside: the agent's assent stays an act, shown as cancelled. Draft 20's G4.10 let a counter signature cancel an assent made in a party's name when made by the assent's signer, under a power of kind `counter_signature`, or as an agreement's signature; draft 21 says, as section 2 already did ("one made in the same party's name"), that the party itself may make it in its own name. Once Terms are in force, only an update (G5.7(a), (b)).
6. **The buyer's Terms, for data** (G11.6(b)). The Terms asked about a report of the buyer's leg are the version the payment names, where an assent of the payer to it is held; and, where the receiving side is reached through another agreement, that agreement's Terms in force too, as G11.6(a) already reads any report that carries the data of people bound by different Terms.
7. **A thief's Terms, not shown and unchecked** (G14.3(b), (c)). His words make the baker, or the holder of the baker's rights, a condition of the agreement's being valid at all. So where the acts held do not yet show the baker or the holder as a party as G14.2 asks, the Terms count for nothing until they do (not shown), and where something they rest on is lacking, every answer resting on them is unchecked. Draft 20 read such Terms by every other grammar rule; that went with its narrower reading. Without this, a thief's offer that reaches the baker only through a role (the baker might fill it), or that names a succession of the thief's own, would still name a receiver.
8. **A later version of a work that drops the baker** (G14.3(a)). It names the envelope without the baker or a succession, so it is invalid, and replaces nothing. A baker leaves a work's Terms only by passing her rights.

**Where the ledger's arrived-at lines say more than the exchanges.** The arrived-at lines are the MOR lead's readings, marked "to check". Draft 21 follows the exchange where a line says more. The full list is in `history.md`, section N.2; the ones that touch what a rule says:

9. **"A late rival": "Where they show it, the update replaces nothing and is no tip of a fork. Where they do not, it is a fork, as draft 20 has it."** These are the MOR lead's lean, which his "Only the knots or anchoring can" answers; his words say what can show the order, and are read as taking the lean. His further words ("the likelihood that they interacted with enough acts to set an order is pretty high"; "We’ll have data after the simulation, 1000 extra identities will be deployed") are not rules, and draft 21 writes nothing for them.
10. **"Setting aside what an agent made": "the grantor's act is an update of what was made in its name".** The MOR lead's, of the counter signature before an agreement is complete. His words do not speak of that case. Draft 21 does not call a counter signature an update: it says what follows (item 5). The earlier lines ("the grantor may set aside an act made under the grant"; the "until complete" lean) are replaced, and not used.
11. **"The buyer signs the offer": "a path beyond the leg for the buyer is whatever the offer's own Terms (their data socket) name".** Followed. Draft 21 also asks, as for any report, the Terms of a receiving side reached through another agreement (item 6), which the line does not say. The MOR lead's withdrawn lean (a buyer who wants to see further signs up to a data path) is not used.
12. **"A thief's Terms": "the grammar is to say it plainly".** Followed (G14.3). His word is "party"; G14.2 asks that the baker, or the holder, be a party "bound by them", an assent counting in their name, as draft 18 read it. Draft 21 keeps that, and asks it now of whether the Terms count at all (item 7; section 8.3, item 8). The first arrived-at line, replaced, said "whoever holds the baker's rights is a party"; the last says "the baker (or the holder of the bakers rights)", as his words do.
13. **The brief's "the counter signature before an agreement is complete".** Read as: before the Terms are in force (G4.10), as draft 20 reads "once all parties have signed".

**What could not be decided from the text at all.** Kept from draft 20, unchanged, under its numbers.

14. **A payment that is pending** (section 9, item 7). No answer word exists for it here. *To settle it:* the Money layer's encoding says whether a payment or a settlement has a pending state, as which act, and what it names; then a cMIP of K6, or the encoding of the answer words (section 10), says what a verifier shows while it lasts. The least reading until then is unchecked (G9).
15. **Money shown as still owed, and later paid** (P20). *To settle it:* the encoding of a leg report's lines (K6's row) gives a "still owed" line something a later line can name (the report it was stated in, and the amount and to whom); then a cMIP of K6 says whether a later report's "passed on" line, naming it, closes it. Until then a "still owed" line is shown as stated and tied to nothing.
16. **Pools** (P6). *To settle it:* a cMIP of K6 for the pool, with a rule as its module, says when it is divided and how a claim is counted; and the encoding of a leg report says how one line names several settlements, or one settlement covers many counts, and whether one report answers several calls (the block's open point 3). Until then one report line names one settlement.
17. **The modules** (a judge, a tally, identity, assurance, delivery, standing; section 5.3). None is a socket. *To settle each:* a cMIP in a socket that names it as a module, and the encoding of what that cMIP is handed and gives back (section 10); for the judge, also where in the Terms it is named (G5.6(c)). Nothing in the grammar needs to change for any of them.

**Section 8.3 of `draft-21.md`: Left as draft 20 has it**

Each item is where a change touches something the four exchanges do not cover. Each is left as draft 20 has it, or says the least that lets the text be read, and says so.

1. **A late act in one signer's own line** (G6.2). The exchange is about updates of Terms. Two acts of one signer that name the same `previous` are a fork, whatever a place shows and whatever they cite, as draft 20 has it: otherwise a signer could hide a fork by citing.
2. **Knots and an anchoring that disagree** (G4.7(c)). Where either shows an update late, it is late. A place that shows an act before an act it names is a place that contradicts the acts; what follows for that place is not said (G7.2: two answers of one place that contradict each other are unchecked).
3. **A clock** (G4.7(b); P16). What a clock is, and whether its answers can order two events, is the layer's that gives it (section 8.1). Until then a late update is late per clock only where the clock does order them.
4. **The update condition beside a late update** (G4.7(d)). An update condition may still answer not valid for an update whose original is no longer the last version (section 5.2); that is unchanged, and now needed only where neither the knots nor the anchoring show it.
5. **An agent's act that is a tip of a fork** (G5.7, G12). It stops, and may be set aside by a resolving update, as any tip, as draft 20 has it. That is the reading of a fork, not the grantor acting on its grant.
6. **A payer whose assent is not held** (G11.6(b)). Where no assent of the payer to the version its payment names is held, or the assent names another version, the payer is not shown as bound by the version paid under, and the answer is no path beyond the leg, as draft 20 had it for every buyer. Whether that should be **not shown** instead (the assent may exist) is not said.
7. **How a buyer's assent stands beside the payment.** Whether they are two acts, or one act can be both, whether the payment is the proof that fills the offer's role (K5), and how both name the same version, is the encoding's and the spot's cMIP's (section 10, item 10).
8. **"Bound by them", for the baker** (G14.2). Kept as draft 20 has it: an assent that counts in the baker's name. So a version of a work that its update condition answers valid without the baker's assent (G5.1(c)) is not shown, and from draft 21 counts for nothing until her assent is shown. Whether "bound" should reach a party bound under G5.1(c) is not said.
9. **A succession that is held and passes nothing** (G14.3(b)). Where every agreement of a named succession is held, and the words of one say nothing of passing the baker's rights, the answer stays **not shown**, as draft 20 has it, and not invalid; from draft 21 those Terms count for nothing while it is so.
10. **A contest against a thief's Terms** (G11.8). A contest names Terms shown as a version; invalid Terms are none, so a contest made against them is not shown. Whether a baker may contest Terms that name her own envelope without her is not said; she may contest Terms for another bake of the same work (G14.5(e)).
11. **The report block** (`report-block-3.md`): not changed, and no fourth proposal (section 8.1).
12. **Everything draft 20 listed as open** in its sections 8.2 and 8.3 is still open, but for what is noted in `history.md`, section N.4. It is summed up in section 8.4.
13. **Nothing was tested against draft 21.** The next step is the encoding and vectors.
